        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.114.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/326/#laws.2.2.0">1989 c 326 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/557/#laws.0.4.0">1994 c 557 s 4</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/86/#laws.3.1.0">1999 c 86 art 3 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/94/#laws.1.10.0">2009 c 94 art 1 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/244/#laws.1.2.0">2012 c 244 art 1 s 2</a>,3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.115"><b>17.115</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.2.87.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 2 s 87</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.116">
        <h1 class="shn">17.116 SUSTAINABLE AGRICULTURE DEMONSTRATION GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.116.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish a grant program for sustainable agriculture methods that demonstrates best management practices, including farm input reduction or management, enterprise diversification including new crops and livestock, farm energy efficiency or production, or the transfer of technologies that enhance the environment and farm profitability. The commissioner shall use the program to demonstrate and publicize the energy efficiency, environmental benefit, and profitability of sustainable agriculture techniques or systems from production through marketing. The grants must fund research or demonstrations on farms consistent with the program objectives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.116.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Grants may be made to farmers and organizations such as farms, agricultural cooperatives, educational institutions, individuals at educational institutions, nonprofit organizations, Tribal governments, or local units of government residing or located in the state for research or demonstrations on farms in the state.</p>
          <p>(b) Grants may only be made for projects that show:</p>
          <p>(1) the ability to maximize direct or indirect energy savings or production;</p>
          <p>(2) a positive effect or reduced adverse effect on the environment; or</p>
          <p>(3) increased profitability for the individual farm by reducing costs or improving marketing opportunities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.116.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Awarding of grants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Applications for grants must be made to the commissioner on forms prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The applications must be reviewed, ranked, and recommended by a technical review panel appointed by the commissioner. The technical review panel shall consist of a soil scientist, an agronomist, a representative from a postsecondary educational institution, an agricultural marketing specialist, two resident farmers of the state using sustainable agriculture methods, two resident farmers of the state using organic agriculture methods, and a chair from the department.</p>
          <p>(c) The technical review panel shall rank applications according to the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) direct or indirect energy savings or production;</p>
          <p>(2) environmental benefit;</p>
          <p>(3) farm profitability;</p>
          <p>(4) the number of farms able to apply the techniques or the technology proposed;</p>
          <p>(5) the effectiveness of the project as a demonstration;</p>
          <p>(6) the immediate transferability of the project to farms; and</p>
          <p>(7) the ability of the project to accomplish its goals.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner shall consider the recommendations of the technical review panel and may award grants for eligible projects. Priority must be given to applicants who are farmers or groups of farmers.</p>
          <p>(e) Grants for eligible projects may not exceed $25,000 unless the portion above $25,000 is matched on an equal basis by the applicant's cash contribution or the value of the applicant's in-kind land use, equipment use, or personal labor. Grant recipients who are not required to provide a match and grant recipients whose in-kind contributions exceed the amount needed to meet matching requirements may submit the value of the grant recipients' labor or equipment use as an expense eligible for payment from grant money. Grant funding of projects may not exceed $50,000 under this section, but applicants may utilize other funding sources. A portion of each grant must be targeted for public information activities of the project.</p>
          <p>(f) A project may continue for up to three years. Multiyear projects must be reevaluated by the technical review panel and the commissioner before second or third year funding is approved. A project is limited to one grant for its funding.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.116.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/688/#laws.15.2.0">1988 c 688 art 15 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.21.0">1997 c 216 s 21</a>,22; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.27.0">1999 c 231 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.24.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/28/#laws.0.4.0">2021 c 28 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/43/#laws.2.7.0">2023 c 43 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/126/#laws.2.7.0">2024 c 126 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.38.7.0">2024 c 127 art 38 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.1161"><b>17.1161</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/181/#laws.0.14.0">2014 c 181 s 14</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">AGRICULTURAL FINANCIAL ASSISTANCE PROGRAMS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.117">
        <h1 class="shn">17.117 AGRICULTURE BEST MANAGEMENT PRACTICES LOAN PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The purpose of the agriculture best management practices loan program is to provide low or no interest financing to farmers, agriculture supply businesses, rural landowners, and water-quality cooperatives for the implementation of agriculture and other best management practices that reduce environmental pollution.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may develop administrative guidelines specifying criteria, standards, and procedures for making loans and establish, adopt rules for, and implement a program to make loans or otherwise provide funds to local units of government, federal authorities, lending institutions, and other appropriate organizations who will in turn provide loans to landowners and businesses for facilities, fixtures, equipment, or other best management practices that prevent or mitigate pollution or other adverse environmental impacts. The agriculture best management practices loan program must provide a consistent programmatic framework for the disbursement and administration of funds available to the commissioner designated to the program for protection of environmental quality or remediation or mitigation of adverse environmental impacts. The distribution of loans or funds through the program must comply with all limitations, provisions, or requirements of the respective funding sources. Unless otherwise limited by the funding source, the commissioner shall manage the program using perpetual revolving fund accounts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appropriations.</span></h2>
          <p>Up to $140,000,000 of the balance in the clean water revolving fund in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/446A.07">446A.07</a>, as determined by the Public Facilities Authority, is appropriated to the commissioner for the establishment of this program. In addition, the commissioner may receive appropriations from the legislature and grants or funds from other sources for implementation of the program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For the purposes of this section, the terms defined in this subdivision have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) "Agricultural and environmental revolving accounts" means accounts in the agricultural fund, controlled by the commissioner, which hold funds available to the program.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) "Agriculture supply business" means a person, partnership, joint venture, corporation, limited liability company, association, firm, public service company, or cooperative that provides materials, equipment, or services to farmers or agriculture-related enterprises.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) "Allocation" means the funds awarded to an applicant for implementation of best management practices through a competitive or noncompetitive application process.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) "Applicant" means a local unit of government eligible to participate in this program that requests an allocation of funds as provided in subdivision 6b.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) "Best management practices" has the meaning given in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.711#stat.103F.711.3">103F.711, subdivision 3</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103H.151#stat.103H.151.2">103H.151, subdivision 2</a>. Best management practices also means other practices, techniques, and measures that have been demonstrated to the satisfaction of the commissioner: (1) to prevent or reduce adverse environmental impacts by using the most effective and practicable means of achieving environmental goals; or (2) to achieve drinking water quality standards under chapter 103H or under Code of Federal Regulations, title 40, parts 141 and 143, as amended.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) "Borrower" means a farmer, an agriculture supply business, or a rural landowner applying for a low-interest loan.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of agriculture, including when the commissioner is acting in the capacity of chair of the Rural Finance Authority, or the designee of the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="in">(i) "Committed project" means an eligible project scheduled to be implemented at a future date that has been approved by the local government unit.</p>
          <p class="in">(j) "Comprehensive water management plan" means a state-approved and locally adopted plan authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.231">103B.231</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.255">103B.255</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.311">103B.311</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103C.331">103C.331</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103D.401">103D.401</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103D.405">103D.405</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(k) "Cost incurred" means expenses for implementation of a project accrued because the borrower has agreed to purchase equipment or is obligated to pay for services or materials already provided as a result of implementing an approved eligible project.</p>
          <p class="in">(l) "Farmer" means a person, partnership, joint venture, corporation, limited liability company, association, firm, public service company, or cooperative that regularly participates in physical labor or operations management of farming and files a Schedule F as part of filing United States Internal Revenue Service Form 1040 or indicates farming as the primary business activity under Schedule C, K, or S, or any other applicable report to the United States Internal Revenue Service.</p>
          <p class="in">(m) "Lender agreement" means an agreement entered into between the commissioner and a local lender which contains terms and conditions of participation in the program.</p>
          <p class="in">(n) "Local government unit" means a county, soil and water conservation district, or an organization formed for the joint exercise of powers under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a> with the authority to participate in the program.</p>
          <p class="in">(o) "Local lender" means a local government unit as defined in paragraph (n); a local unit of government with taxing or special assessment authority, such as a watershed district, a drainage authority, or a township; a state or federally chartered bank; a savings association; a state or federal credit union; Agribank and its affiliated organizations; or a nonprofit economic development organization or other financial lending institution approved by the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="in">(p) "Local revolving loan account" means the account held by a local government unit and a local lender into which principal repayments from borrowers are deposited and new loans are issued in accordance with the requirements of the program and lender agreements.</p>
          <p class="in">(q) "Nonpoint source" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.711#stat.103F.711.6">103F.711, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(r) "Program" means the agriculture best management practices loan program in this section.</p>
          <p class="in">(s) "Project" means one or more components or activities located within Minnesota that are required by the local government unit to be implemented for satisfactory completion of an eligible best management practice.</p>
          <p class="in">(t) "Rural landowner" means the owner of record of Minnesota real estate located in an area determined by the local government unit to be rural after consideration of local land use patterns, zoning regulations, jurisdictional boundaries, local community definitions, historical uses, and other pertinent local factors.</p>
          <p class="in">(u) "Water-quality cooperative" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.58">115.58</a>, paragraph (d), except as expressly limited in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Uses of funds.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Use of funds under this section must be in compliance with the rules and regulations of the funding source or appropriation. Use of funds from the Public Facilities Authority must comply with the federal Water Pollution Control Act, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/446A.07">446A.07</a>, and eligible activities listed in the intended use plan authorized in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/446A.07#stat.446A.07.4">446A.07, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) In the event of a conflict between this section and a law appropriating money for this program, the law appropriating money for this program governs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Agricultural and environmental revolving accounts.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) There shall be established in the special revenue fund revolving accounts to receive appropriations, transfers of the balances from previous appropriations for the activities under this section, and money from other sources. All balances from previous appropriations for activities under this section and repayments of loans granted under this section, including principal and interest, must be deposited into the appropriate revolving account created in this subdivision or the account created in subdivision 13. Interest earned in an account accrues to that account.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The money in the revolving accounts and the account created in subdivision 13 is appropriated to the commissioner for the purposes of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.5b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5b.<span class="headnote">Application fee.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may impose a nonrefundable application fee of $50 for each loan issued under the program. The fees must be credited to the agricultural best management practices administration account, which is hereby established in the special revenue fund. Interest earned in the account accrues to the account. Money in the account and interest earned in the accounts established in the agricultural fund under subdivision 5a are appropriated to the commissioner for administrative expenses of the program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Only the following local government units may apply for funds under this program:</p>
          <p>(1) counties or their designees;</p>
          <p>(2) soil and water conservation districts; and</p>
          <p>(3) joint power organizations consisting of counties or their designees or soil and water conservation districts.</p>
          <p>(b) A county may submit an application for an allocation. A county or a group of counties may designate another local government unit to submit a local allocation request on their behalf. If a county does not submit an application, and does not designate another local government unit, a soil and water conservation district may submit an application for an allocation. If the local soil and water conservation district does not submit an application, then an eligible joint powers organization may submit an application for an allocation. In all instances, there may be only one application representing any geographic area. The applicant must coordinate and submit requests on behalf of other units of government within the geographic jurisdiction of the applicant.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner must prescribe forms and establish an application process for applicants to apply for an allocation of funds. The application must include but need not be limited to (1) the geographic area served; (2) the type and estimated cost of activities or projects for which they are seeking an allocation; and (3) prioritization or targeting of proposed activities or projects.</p>
          <p>(d) If an application is rejected, the applicant must be notified in writing as to the reasons for the rejection and given 30 days to submit a revised application. The revised application shall be reviewed according to the same procedure used to review the initial application. Failure of an applicant to be awarded funds does not constitute a rejection of the application.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Review and ranking of applications.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall chair a subcommittee for purposes of reviewing and ranking applications and recommending to the commissioner allocation amounts. The subcommittee consists of representatives of the Departments of Agriculture, Natural Resources, and Health; the Pollution Control Agency; the Board of Water and Soil Resources; the Farm Service Agency and the Natural Resources Conservation Service of the United States Department of Agriculture; the Association of Minnesota Counties; the Minnesota Association of Soil and Water Conservation Districts; and other agencies or associations the commissioner determines are appropriate.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The subcommittee must use the criteria in clauses (1) to (9) as well as other criteria it determines appropriate in carrying out the review and ranking:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) whether the proposed activities are identified in a comprehensive water management plan or other appropriate local planning documents as priorities;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the potential that the proposed activities have for improving or protecting environmental quality;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the extent that the proposed activities support areawide or multijurisdictional approaches to protecting environmental quality based on defined watershed or similar geographic areas;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) whether the activities are needed for compliance with existing environmental laws or rules;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) whether the proposed activities demonstrate participation, coordination, and cooperation between local units of government and other public agencies;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) whether there is coordination with other public and private funding sources and programs;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) whether the applicant has targeted specific best management practices to resolve specific environmental problems;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) past performance of the applicant in completing projects identified in prior applications and allocation agreements; and</p>
          <p class="in">(9) whether there are off-site public benefits.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.6b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6b.<span class="headnote">Allocation amount.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The subcommittee created in subdivision 6a shall recommend to the commissioner the amount of allocation for each applicant. This allocation must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the amount of repayments received by the commissioner during the previous year from prior completed projects approved by the local government unit; and</p>
          <p>(2) the amount of funds previously designated to committed projects.</p>
          <p>(b) Within the limits of the funds available to the commissioner, the subcommittee may recommend an increased allocation award to the applicant based on:</p>
          <p>(1) the ranking of the local government unit application under subdivision 6a; and</p>
          <p>(2) the amount of unallocated or uncommitted funds in, or that will be received by, the agricultural and environmental revolving accounts within one year.</p>
          <p>(c) Notwithstanding paragraphs (a) and (b), the commissioner may reserve up to two percent of all funds appropriated to the agricultural and environmental revolving accounts to be allocated to applicants that disburse or commit all of their current allocations or to local lenders who wish to provide financial assistance.</p>
          <p>The commissioner may add, for the purposes of calculating future allocations under paragraphs (a) and (b), the loan amount for projects financed from these reserved funds to the allocation for the respective local government units in which jurisdiction the project was completed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Payments to local lenders.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Payments made from the commissioner to the local lender must be made in accordance with applicable state and federal laws and rules governing the payments and the lender agreement.</p>
          <p>(b) Payments from the commissioner to the local lender must be disbursed on a cost-incurred basis. The request must be made in accordance with requirements and procedures established by the commissioner. Payment requests must be reviewed and approved by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Allocation agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Eligible local government units with an allocation award may enter into an allocation agreement with the commissioner and participate in this program.</p>
          <p>(b) The allocation agreement must contain terms and conditions for participation in this program and providing of funds through this program, including, but not limited to: program requirements, reporting requirements, project eligibility and limitations, allowable expenses, limitations, rescission and cancellation provisions, and the responsibilities of the commissioner, local government unit, and local lender.</p>
          <p>(c) If the commissioner determines that a local government unit is not in compliance with the terms of the allocation agreement, the commissioner may rescind all or part of any allocation awarded through this program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Allocation rescission.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Continued availability of allocations granted to a local government unit is contingent upon the commissioner's approval of the local government unit's annual report. The commissioner shall review this annual report to ensure that the past and future uses of the funds are consistent with the comprehensive water management plan, other local planning documents, the requirements of the funding source, and compliance to program requirements. If the commissioner concludes the past or intended uses of the money are not consistent with these requirements, the commissioner shall rescind all or part of the allocation awarded to a local government unit.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may rescind funds allocated to the local government unit that are not designated to committed projects or disbursed within one year from the date of the allocation agreement.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may rescind uncommitted allocations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.9a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9a.<span class="headnote">Authority and responsibilities of local government units.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A local government unit that enters into an allocation agreement with the commissioner:</p>
          <p>(1) is responsible for the local administration and implementation of the program in accordance with this section;</p>
          <p>(2) may submit applications for allocations to the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(3) shall identify, develop, determine eligibility, define and approve projects, designate maximum loan amounts for projects, and certify completion of projects implemented under this program. In areas where no local government unit has applied for funds under this program, the commissioner may appoint a local government unit to review and certify projects or the commissioner may assume the authority and responsibility of the local government unit;</p>
          <p>(4) shall certify as eligible only projects that are within its geographic jurisdiction or within the geographic area identified in its local comprehensive water management plans or other local planning documents;</p>
          <p>(5) may require withholding by the local lender of all or a portion of the loan to the borrower until satisfactory completion of all required components of a certified project;</p>
          <p>(6) shall report to the commissioner annually the past and intended uses of allocations awarded; and</p>
          <p>(7) may request additional funds in excess of their allocation when funds are available in the agricultural and environmental revolving accounts, as long as all other allocation awards to the local government unit have been used or committed.</p>
          <p>(b) If a local government unit withdraws from participation in this program, the local government unit, or the commissioner in accordance with the priorities established under subdivision 6a, may designate another local government unit that is eligible under subdivision 6 as the new local government unit responsible for local administration of this program. This designated local government unit may accept responsibility and administration of allocations awarded to the former responsible local government unit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.9b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9b.<span class="headnote">Lender agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Any local lender entering into a lender agreement with the commissioner may participate in this program.</p>
          <p>(b) The lender agreement will contain terms and conditions for participation in this program and providing funds to the local lenders, including but not limited to, program requirements, loan and account management requirements, payments, repayments, term limits, allowable expenses, fee limitations, rescission and cancellation provisions, collateral and security requirements, reporting requirements, review and appeal procedure for cancellation of the loan agreement or disqualification as a local lender, and the responsibilities of the commissioner, local government unit, and local lender.</p>
          <p>(c) If the commissioner determines that a local lender is not in compliance with the terms of the lender agreement, the commissioner may take the following actions:</p>
          <p>(1) disqualifying the local lender as a participating lender in this program for a period of up to five years from the date that the commissioner determines noncompliance to the lender agreement; and</p>
          <p>(2) requiring immediate or accelerated repayment of all or part of all funds provided to the local lender.</p>
          <p>(d) Existing lender agreements, executed prior to July 1, 2001, may be amended by mutual consent of all signatory parties, to comply with this section, to establish a single allocation agreement that includes the amount of prior allocation awards and defines the terms and conditions required under subdivision 8, or to modify the amount of allocation awarded.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Authority and responsibilities of local lenders.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Local lenders may enter into lender agreements with the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) Local lenders may enter into loan agreements with borrowers to finance eligible projects under this section.</p>
          <p>(c) Local lenders with local revolving loan accounts created before July 1, 2001, may continue to retain and use those accounts in accordance with their lending agreements for the full term of those agreements.</p>
          <p>(d) Local lenders, including local government units designating themselves as the local lender, may enter into participation agreements with other lenders.</p>
          <p>(e) Local lenders may enter into contracts with other lenders for the limited purposes of loan review, processing and servicing, or to enter into loan agreements with borrowers to finance projects under this section. Other lenders entering into contracts with local lenders under this section must meet the definition of local lender in subdivision 4, must comply with all provisions of the lender agreement and this section, and must guarantee repayment of the loan funds to the local lender.</p>
          <p>(f) When required by the local government unit, a local lender must withhold all or a portion of the loan disbursement for a project until notified by the local government unit that the project has been satisfactorily completed.</p>
          <p>(g) The local lender is responsible for repaying all funds provided by the commissioner to the local lender.</p>
          <p>(h) The local lender is responsible for collecting repayments from borrowers. If a borrower defaults on a loan issued by the local lender, it is the responsibility of the local lender to obtain repayment from the borrower. Default on the part of borrowers shall have no effect on the local lender's responsibility to repay its obligations to the commissioner whether or not the local lender fully recovers defaulted amounts from borrowers.</p>
          <p>(i) The local lender shall provide sufficient collateral or protection to the commissioner for the funds provided to the local lender. The commissioner must approve the collateral or protection provided.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Loans issued to borrower.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Local lenders may issue loans only for projects that are approved and certified by the local government unit as meeting priority needs identified in a comprehensive water management plan or other local planning documents, are in compliance with accepted practices, standards, specifications, or criteria, and are eligible for financing under Environmental Protection Agency or other applicable guidelines.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The local lender may use any additional criteria considered necessary to determine the eligibility of borrowers for loans.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Local lenders shall set the terms and conditions of loans to borrowers, except that no borrower shall, at any time, have loans from this program with a total outstanding loan balance of more than $200,000.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The maximum term length for projects in this paragraph is ten years.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) Fees charged at the time of closing must:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) be in compliance with normal and customary practices of the local lender;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) be in accordance with published fee schedules issued by the local lender;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) not be based on participation program; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) be consistent with fees charged other similar types of loans offered by the local lender.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) The interest rate assessed to an outstanding loan balance by the local lender must not exceed three percent per year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.11a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11a.<span class="headnote">Eligible projects.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) All projects that remediate or mitigate adverse environmental impacts are eligible if the project is eligible under an allocation agreement.</p>
          <p>(b) A manure management project is eligible if the project remediates or mitigates impacts from facilities with less than 1,000 animal units as defined in Minnesota Rules, chapter 7020, and otherwise meets the requirements of this section.</p>
          <p>(c) A drinking water project is eligible if the project:</p>
          <p>(1) remediates or mitigates the inadequate flow, adverse environmental impacts, or presence of contaminants in privately owned water supplies that are used for drinking water by people or livestock, privately owned water service lines, or privately owned plumbing and fixtures;</p>
          <p>(2) implements best management practices that are intended to achieve drinking water standards or adequate flow; and</p>
          <p>(3) otherwise meets the requirements of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Data privacy.</span></h2>
          <p>The following data on local government units, local lenders, or borrowers collected by the commissioner under this section are private for data on individuals as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.12">13.02, subdivision 12</a>, or nonpublic for data not on individuals as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.9">13.02, subdivision 9</a>: financial information, including, but not limited to, credit reports, financial statements, tax returns and net worth calculations received or prepared by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Establishment of account.</span></h2>
          <p>The Public Facilities Authority shall establish an account called the agriculture best management practices revolving account to provide loans and other forms of financial assistance authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/446A.07">446A.07</a>. The account must be credited with repayments.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.25.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 25</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's report.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall prepare and submit a report to the house of representatives and senate committees with jurisdiction over the environment, natural resources, and agriculture by October 15 of each odd-numbered year.</p>
          <p>(b) The report shall include, but need not be limited to, matters such as loan allocations and uses, the extent to which the financial assistance is helping implement local water and other environmental planning priorities, the integration or coordination that has occurred with related programs, and other matters deemed pertinent to the implementation of the program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Liens against property.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The amount of loans and accruing interest made by a county, home rule charter city, statutory city, or town acting as a local lender under this section is a lien against the real property for which the improvement was made and must be assessed against the property or properties benefited unless the amount is prepaid. The lien is a special assessment under chapter 429 and repayments may be collected as a special assessment as provided for in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/429.101">429.101</a> or by charter. An amount loaned under the program and its accruing interest assessed against the property is a priority lien only against subsequent liens.</p>
          <p>(b) The county, home rule charter city, statutory city, or town may bill amounts due on the loan on the tax statement for the property. Enforcement of the lien created by this subdivision must, at the county's, home rule charter city's, statutory city's, or town's option, be in the manner set forth in chapter 580 or 581. When the amount due and all interest has been paid, the county, home rule charter city, statutory city, or town shall file a satisfaction of the lien created under this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(c) A county, home rule charter city, statutory city, or town may also secure amounts due on a loan under this section by taking a purchase money security interest in equipment in accordance with chapter 336, article 9, and may enforce the purchase money security interest in accordance with chapters 336, article 9, and 565.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.117.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Referendum exemption.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purpose of obtaining a loan from the commissioner, a local government unit acting as a local lender may provide to the commissioner its general obligation note. All obligations incurred by a local government unit in obtaining a loan from the commissioner must be in accordance with chapter 475, except that so long as the obligations are issued to evidence a loan from the commissioner to the local government unit, an election is not required to authorize the obligations issued, and the amount of the obligations shall not be included in determining the net indebtedness of the local government unit under the provisions of any law or chapter limiting the indebtedness.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.117.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/632/#laws.2.11.0">1994 c 632 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/202/#laws.1.25.0">1995 c 202 art 1 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/220/#laws.0.28.0">1995 c 220 s 28</a>-39; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/407/#laws.0.12.0">1996 c 407 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/86/#laws.3.3.0">1999 c 86 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.28.0">1999 c 231 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.25.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/220/#laws.9.5.0">2002 c 220 art 9 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.8.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/45/#laws.1.15.0">2007 c 45 art 1 s 15</a>-19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/277/#laws.3.1.0">2008 c 277 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.1.0">2011 c 107 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/44/#laws.0.2.0">2015 c 44 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.2.6.0">2016 c 189 art 2 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/24/#laws.0.1.0">2019 c 24 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/89/#laws.4.3.0">2020 c 89 art 4 s 3</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/95/#laws.2.3.0">2022 c 95 art 2 s 3</a>-7</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.118">
        <h1 class="shn">17.118 LIVESTOCK INVESTMENT GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.118.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may award a livestock investment grant to a person who raises livestock in this state equal to ten percent of the first $500,000 of qualifying expenditures, provided the person makes qualifying expenditures of at least $4,000. The commissioner may award multiple livestock investment grants to a person over the life of the program and shall give preference to applicants who have not previously received a grant under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.118.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For the purposes of this section, the terms defined in this subdivision have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) "Livestock" means beef cattle, dairy cattle, swine, poultry, goats, mules, farmed Cervidae, Ratitae, bison, sheep, horses, and llamas.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) "Qualifying expenditures" means the amount spent for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the acquisition, construction, or improvement of buildings or facilities for the production of livestock or livestock products;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the development of pasture for use by livestock including, but not limited to, the acquisition, development, or improvement of:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) lanes used by livestock that connect pastures to a central location;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) watering systems for livestock on pasture including water lines, booster pumps, and well installations;</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) livestock stream crossing stabilization; and</p>
          <p class="in">(iv) fences; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the acquisition of equipment for livestock housing, confinement, feeding, and waste management including, but not limited to, the following:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) freestall barns;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) watering facilities;</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) feed storage and handling equipment;</p>
          <p class="in">(iv) milking parlors;</p>
          <p class="in">(v) robotic equipment;</p>
          <p class="in">(vi) scales;</p>
          <p class="in">(vii) milk storage and cooling facilities;</p>
          <p class="in">(viii) bulk tanks;</p>
          <p class="in">(ix) computer hardware and software and associated equipment used to monitor the productivity and feeding of livestock;</p>
          <p class="in">(x) manure pumping and storage facilities;</p>
          <p class="in">(xi) swine farrowing facilities;</p>
          <p class="in">(xii) swine and cattle finishing barns;</p>
          <p class="in">(xiii) calving facilities;</p>
          <p class="in">(xiv) digesters;</p>
          <p class="in">(xv) equipment used to produce energy;</p>
          <p class="in">(xvi) on-farm processing facilities equipment;</p>
          <p class="in">(xvii) fences, including but not limited to farmed Cervidae perimeter fences required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/35.155#stat.35.155.4">35.155, subdivision 4</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(xviii) livestock pens and corrals and sorting, restraining, and loading chutes.</p>
          <p class="in">Except for qualifying pasture development expenditures under clause (2), qualifying expenditures only include amounts that are allowed to be capitalized and deducted under either section 167 or 179 of the Internal Revenue Code in computing federal taxable income. Qualifying expenditures do not include an amount paid to refinance existing debt.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.118.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p class="in"> To be eligible for a livestock investment grant, a person must:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) be a resident of Minnesota or an entity specifically defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/500.24">500.24</a>, subdivision 2, that is eligible to own farmland and operate a farm in this state under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/500.24">500.24</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) be the principal operator of the farm;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) hold a feedlot registration, if required; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) apply to the commissioner on forms prescribed by the commissioner including a statement of the qualifying expenditures made during the qualifying period along with any proof or other documentation the commissioner may require.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.118.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Process.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner, in consultation with the chairs and ranking minority members of the house of representatives and senate committees with jurisdiction over agriculture finance, shall develop competitive eligibility criteria and may allocate grants on a needs basis. The commissioner shall place any eligible unfunded applications on a waiting list and, notwithstanding subdivision 2, paragraph (d), give them consideration during the next fiscal year in which program funding is available. The commissioner shall notify in writing any applicant who applies for a grant and is ineligible under the provisions of this section as well as any applicant whose application is received or reviewed after the fiscal year funding limit has been reached.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.118.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/297/#laws.1.1.0">2008 c 297 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/94/#laws.1.12.0">2009 c 94 art 1 s 12</a>,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.2.4.0">2013 c 114 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/1/#laws.2.2.0">1Sp2019 c 1 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/95/#laws.2.8.0">2022 c 95 art 2 s 8</a>,9</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.119"><b>17.119</b> MS 2018 [Expired, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.2.8.0">2016 c 189 art 2 s 8</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.1195">
        <h1 class="shn">17.1195 FARM SAFETY GRANT AND OUTREACH PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.1195.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Tractor rollover grants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may award grants to Minnesota farmers and Minnesota schools that retrofit eligible tractors with eligible rollover protective structures.</p>
          <p>(b) Grants for farmers are limited to 70 percent of the farmer's documented cost to purchase, ship, and install an eligible rollover protective structure. The commissioner must increase a farmer's grant award amount over the 70 percent grant limitation requirement if necessary to limit a farmer's cost per tractor to no more than $500.</p>
          <p>(c) Schools are eligible for grants that cover the full amount of a school's documented cost to purchase, ship, and install an eligible rollover protective structure.</p>
          <p>(d) A rollover protective structure is eligible if it is certified to appropriate national or international rollover protection structure standards with a seat belt.</p>
          <p>(e) "Eligible tractor" means a tractor that was built before 1987.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.1195.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grain storage facility safety grants; farm safety outreach.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may award grants to Minnesota farmers who purchase eligible grain storage facility safety equipment. Grants are limited to 75 percent of the farmer's documented cost to purchase, ship, and install grain storage facility safety equipment, or $400 per bin or silo, whichever is less. A farm entity may not receive more than one grant each fiscal year.</p>
          <p>(b) Eligible grain storage facility safety equipment includes:</p>
          <p>(1) fall protection systems;</p>
          <p>(2) engineering controls to prevent contact with an auger or other moving parts;</p>
          <p>(3) dust collection systems to minimize explosion hazards;</p>
          <p>(4) personal protective equipment to increase survivability in the event of a grain-bin-related emergency;</p>
          <p>(5) grain silo air quality monitoring equipment; and</p>
          <p>(6) other grain storage facility safety equipment approved by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may create a farm safety outreach campaign, including but not limited to development and distribution of safety educational materials related to grain bins, silos, and other agricultural confined spaces.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.1195.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Promotion; administration.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may spend up to six percent of total program dollars each fiscal year to promote and administer the programs to Minnesota farmers and schools.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.1195.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/101/#laws.0.1.0">2020 c 101 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.12"><b>17.12</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/92/#laws.0.3.0">1955 c 92 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.13"><b>17.13</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/92/#laws.0.3.0">1955 c 92 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.133">
        <h1 class="shn">17.133 FARM DOWN PAYMENT ASSISTANCE GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.133.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Eligible farmer" means an individual who at the time that the grant is awarded:</p>
          <p>(1) is a resident of Minnesota who intends to acquire farmland located within the state and provide the majority of the day-to-day physical labor and management of the farm;</p>
          <p>(2) grosses no more than $250,000 per year from the sale of farm products;</p>
          <p>(3) has not, and whose spouse has not, at any time had a direct or indirect ownership interest in farmland; and</p>
          <p>(4) is not, and whose spouse is not, related by blood or marriage to an owner of the farmland that the individual intends to acquire.</p>
          <p>(c) "Farm down payment" means an initial, partial payment required by a lender or seller to purchase farmland.</p>
          <p>(d) "Incubator farm" means a farm where:</p>
          <p>(1) individuals are given temporary, exclusive, and affordable access to small parcels of land, infrastructure, and often training, for the purpose of honing skills and launching a farm business; and</p>
          <p>(2) a majority of the individuals farming the small parcels of land grow industrial hemp, cannabis, or one or more of the following specialty crops as defined by the United States Department of Agriculture for purposes of the specialty crop block grant program: fruits and vegetables, tree nuts, dried fruits, medicinal plants, culinary herbs and spices, horticulture crops, floriculture crops, and nursery crops.</p>
          <p>(e) "Limited land access" means farming without ownership of land and:</p>
          <p>(1) the individual or the individual's child rents or leases the land, with the term of each rental or lease agreement not exceeding three years in duration, from a person who is not related to the individual or the individual's spouse by blood or marriage; or</p>
          <p>(2) the individual rents the land from an incubator farm.</p>
          <p>(f) "Limited market access" means the individual has gross sales of no more than $100,000 per year from the sale of farm products.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.133.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may award farm down payment assistance grants of up to $15,000 per eligible farmer. Each award must be matched with at least $8,000 of other funding. Grants under this subdivision may be awarded by a randomized selection process after applications are collected over a period of no less than 30 calendar days. An eligible farmer must commit to own and farm the land purchased with assistance provided under this section for at least five years. For each year that a grant recipient does not own and farm the land during the five-year period, the grant recipient must pay a penalty to the commissioner equal to 20 percent of the grant amount.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.133.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Report to legislature.</span></h2>
          <p>No later than December 1, 2023, and annually thereafter, the commissioner must provide a report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees having jurisdiction over agriculture and rural development, in compliance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.195">3.195</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.197">3.197</a>, on the farm down payment assistance grants under this section. The report must include:</p>
          <p>(1) background information on beginning farmers in Minnesota and any other information that the commissioner and authority find relevant to evaluating the effect of the grants on increasing opportunities for and the number of beginning farmers;</p>
          <p>(2) the number and amount of grants;</p>
          <p>(3) the geographic distribution of grants by county;</p>
          <p>(4) the number of grant recipients who are emerging farmers;</p>
          <p>(5) the number of grant recipients who were experiencing limited land access or limited market access when the grant was awarded;</p>
          <p>(6) disaggregated data regarding the gender, race, and ethnicity of grant recipients;</p>
          <p>(7) the number of farmers who cease to own land and are subject to payment of a penalty, along with the reasons for the land ownership cessation; and</p>
          <p>(8) the number and amount of grant applications that exceeded the allocation available in each year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.133.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/95/#laws.2.10.0">2022 c 95 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/43/#laws.2.8.0">2023 c 43 art 2 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/126/#laws.2.8.0">2024 c 126 art 2 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.38.8.0">2024 c 127 art 38 s 8</a>,9</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.134">
        <h1 class="shn">17.134 SOIL HEALTH FINANCIAL ASSISTANCE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.134.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must establish and administer a program to support healthy soil management practices in accordance with this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.134.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible projects.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may award a grant under this section for any project on agricultural land in Minnesota that will:</p>
          <p>(1) increase the quantity of organic carbon in soil through practices, including but not limited to reduced tillage, cover cropping, manure management, precision agriculture, crop rotations, and changes in grazing management;</p>
          <p>(2) integrate perennial vegetation into the management of agricultural lands;</p>
          <p>(3) reduce nitrous oxide and methane emissions through changes to livestock, soil management, or nutrient optimization;</p>
          <p>(4) increase the usage of precision agricultural practices;</p>
          <p>(5) enable the development of site-specific management plans; or</p>
          <p>(6) enable the purchase of equipment, parts and materials, technology, subscriptions, technical assistance, seeds, seedlings, or amendments that will further any of the purposes in clauses (1) to (5).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.134.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grant eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>Any owner or lessee of farmland may apply for a grant under this section. The commissioner must give preference to owners and lessees that have not previously implemented an eligible project and owners and lessees that are certified or assessed and pursuing certification under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.9891">17.9891</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.993">17.993</a>. Local government units, including cities; towns; counties; soil and water conservation districts; Minnesota Tribal governments as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10.65">10.65</a>; and joint powers boards, are also eligible for a grant. A local government unit that receives a grant for equipment or technology must make those purchases available for use by the public.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.134.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Equipment sales limitation.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to the applicable grants management requirements imposed under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.97">16B.97</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.991">16B.991</a>, an owner or lessee that receives a grant under this section to purchase equipment must certify to the commissioner that the owner or lessee will not sell the equipment for at least ten years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.134.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 15 each year, the commissioner must submit a report on the grants awarded under this section to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees and divisions with jurisdiction over agriculture policy and finance. The report must include the number of grants awarded by county and the combined value of those grants.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.134.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/43/#laws.2.10.0">2023 c 43 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/126/#laws.2.10.0">2024 c 126 art 2 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.38.10.0">2024 c 127 art 38 s 10</a>,11</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">FARM SOLID WASTE DISPOSAL</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.135">
        <h1 class="shn">17.135 FARM DISPOSAL OF SOLID WASTE.</h1>
        <p>(a) A permit is not required from a state agency, except under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/88.16">88.16</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/88.17">88.17</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/88.22">88.22</a> for a person who owns or operates land used for farming that buries, or burns and buries:</p>
        <p>(1) solid waste generated from the person's household or as part of the person's farming operation; or</p>
        <p>(2) concrete or reinforcing bar from a building or structure located on the land used for farming.</p>
        <p>Items in clauses (1) and (2) must be buried in a nuisance-free, pollution-free, and aesthetic manner on the land used for farming. The exception in clause (1) does not apply if regularly scheduled pickup of solid waste is reasonably available at the person's farm, as determined by resolution of the county board of the county where the person's farm is located.</p>
        <p>(b) The exemption in paragraph (a), clause (1), does not apply to burning tires or plastics, except plastic baling twine, or to burning or burial of the following materials:</p>
        <p>(1) household hazardous waste as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.96#stat.115A.96.1">115A.96, subdivision 1</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) appliances, including but not limited to, major appliances as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03#stat.115A.03.17a">115A.03, subdivision 17a</a>;</p>
        <p>(3) household batteries;</p>
        <p>(4) used motor oil; and</p>
        <p>(5) lead acid batteries from motor vehicles.</p>
        <p>(c) Within 90 days after completion of the burial, an owner of land used for farming who buries material under the authority of paragraph (a), clause (2), shall record, with the county recorder or registrar of titles of the county in which the land is located, an affidavit containing a legal description of the property and a map drawn from available information showing the boundary of the property and the location of concrete or reinforcing bar buried on the property. The county recorder or registrar of titles must record an affidavit presented under this paragraph in a manner that ensures its disclosure in the ordinary course of a title search of the subject property.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.135.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/131/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 131 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 249 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/14/#laws.0.6.0">2011 c 14 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.136"><b>17.136</b> MS 2006 [Expired, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.7.0">2001 c 161 s 7</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.138"><b>17.138</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/181/#laws.0.14.0">2014 c 181 s 14</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.139"><b>17.139</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.162.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 162</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.17.14">
        <h1>17.14 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.14.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.34"><b>17.34</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/73/#laws.0.1.0">Ex1959 c 73 s 1</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">AGRICULTURAL PURSUIT NOT DETERMINATIVE FOR PROPERTY CLASSIFICATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.345">
        <h1 class="shn">17.345 NOT DETERMINATIVE FOR PROPERTY CLASSIFICATION.</h1>
        <p>Participating in an agricultural pursuit identified in this chapter is not determinative for the classification of property under chapter 273.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.345.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/203/#laws.0.1.0">2012 c 203 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">FUR FARMING</h1>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.35"><b>17.35</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/44/#laws.0.5.0">1985 c 44 s 5</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.351">
        <h1 class="shn">17.351 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.351.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.352">17.352</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.354">17.354</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.351.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.351.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Fur-bearing animal.</span></h2>
          <p>"Fur-bearing animal" means a fox, mink, fitch, chinchilla, karakul, marten, nutria, or fisher that is the second or later generation raised in captivity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.351.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Fur farmer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Fur farmer" means a person engaged in breeding, raising, producing, and marketing fur-bearing animals or the products of fur-bearing animals.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.351.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/44/#laws.0.1.0">1985 c 44 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.352">
        <h1 class="shn">17.352 AGRICULTURAL PRODUCTS AND PURSUITS.</h1>
        <p>Fur-bearing animals are domestic animals and products of fur-bearing animals are agricultural products. A fur farmer is engaged in an agricultural pursuit.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.352.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/44/#laws.0.2.0">1985 c 44 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.353"><b>17.353</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.6.7.0">2024 c 116 art 6 s 7</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.354">
        <h1 class="shn">17.354 APPLICATION.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.351">17.351</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.353">17.353</a> do not affect provisions of law relating to wild animals.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.354.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/44/#laws.0.4.0">1985 c 44 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.36"><b>17.36</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/31.73">31.73</a>]
</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.37"><b>17.37</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/128/#laws.0.20.0">1961 c 128 s 20</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CHEMICALLY TREATED GRAIN</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.41">
        <h1 class="shn">17.41 GRAIN DEFINED.</h1>
        <p>Unless the language or context clearly indicates that a different meaning is intended, the term "grain" shall mean whole seeds or entire grains or any broken parts thereof, of field peas, field beans, soybeans or entire legumes, or flax, wheat, rye, barley, oats, corn, sorghum, spelt or emmer, vetch, buckwheat, or any other cereals.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.41.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/415/#laws.0.1.0">1955 c 415 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.42"><b>17.42</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/181/#laws.0.14.0">2014 c 181 s 14</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.43"><b>17.43</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/181/#laws.0.14.0">2014 c 181 s 14</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.44"><b>17.44</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/181/#laws.0.14.0">2014 c 181 s 14</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">BEE INSPECTION FEES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.445">
        <h1 class="shn">17.445 INSPECTIONS AND SERVICES; FEES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.445.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, the definitions in this subdivision have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(a) "Apiary" means a place where a collection of one or more hives or colonies of bees or the nuclei of bees are kept.</p>
          <p>(b) "Bee equipment" means hives, supers, frames, veils, gloves, and any apparatus, tool, machine, vehicle, or other device used in the handling, moving, or manipulating of bees, honey, wax, or hives, including containers of honey or wax, which may be used in an apiary or in transporting bees and their products and apiary supplies.</p>
          <p>(c) "Bees" means any stage of the common honey bee, Apis mellifera (L).</p>
          <p>(d) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of agriculture or the commissioner's designees or authorized agents.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.445.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>To ensure continued access to foreign and domestic markets, the commissioner shall provide requested bee inspections and other necessary services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.445.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Inspections and other services.</span></h2>
          <p>On request, the commissioner may make inspections for sale of bees, bee equipment, or appliances or perform other necessary services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.445.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Fees.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall charge a fee or charge for expenses so as to recover the cost of performing the inspections and services in subdivision 3. If a person for whom these inspections or services are to be performed requests it, the commissioner shall provide to the person in advance an estimate of the fees or expenses that will be charged. All fees and charges collected under this section shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the agricultural fund. Revenue from inspection fees and other charges deposited in the agricultural fund, including any interest earned, is appropriated to the commissioner to perform the services provided for under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.445.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/265/#laws.0.1.0">2006 c 265 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.45"><b>17.45</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.162.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 162</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">FARMED CERVIDAE</h1>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.451"><b>17.451</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.98.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 98</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.452">
        <h1 class="shn">17.452 FARM-RAISED CERVIDAE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/181/#laws.0.14.0">2014 c 181 s 14</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/181/#laws.0.14.0">2014 c 181 s 14</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.2.67.0">1997 c 7 art 2 s 67</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Farmed Cervidae are livestock.</span></h2>
          <p>Farmed Cervidae are livestock and are not wild animals for purposes of game farm, hunting, or wildlife laws. Farmed Cervidae and their products are farm products and livestock for purposes of financial transactions and collateral.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Raising farmed Cervidae is an agricultural pursuit.</span></h2>
          <p>Raising farmed Cervidae is agricultural production and an agricultural pursuit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Other applicable definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/35.153">35.153</a> apply to this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.98.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.98.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.98.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Slaughter.</span></h2>
          <p>Farmed Cervidae must be slaughtered and inspected in accordance with chapters 31 and 31A or the United States Department of Agriculture voluntary program for exotic animals, Code of Federal Regulations, title 9, part 352.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Sales of farmed Cervidae and meat products.</span></h2>
          <p>Persons selling or buying farmed Cervidae sold as livestock, sold for human consumption, or sold for slaughter must comply with chapters 17A, 31, 31A, and 31B.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.98.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.98.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.98.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.98.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.13a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.98.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.98.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.98.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.452.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.98.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.452.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
