        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.9.2.0">1993 c 375 art 9 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/39/#laws.0.2.0">1995 c 39 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/86/#laws.2.1.0">1999 c 86 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/373/#laws.0.2.0">2002 c 373 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.3.6.0">2003 c 128 art 3 s 6</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.10.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">RATITAE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.453">
        <h1 class="shn">17.453 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.453.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.454">17.454</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.453.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Owner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Owner" means a person who owns or is responsible for the raising of Ratitae.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.453.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Ratitae.</span></h2>
          <p>"Ratitae" means members of the Ratitae family (including ostriches, emus, and rheas) that are raised for the purpose of producing fiber, meat, or animal by-products or as breeding stock.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.453.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.9.3.0">1993 c 375 art 9 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.454">
        <h1 class="shn">17.454 RATITAE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.454.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Ratitae are livestock.</span></h2>
          <p>Ratitae are livestock and are not wild animals for purposes of hunting or wildlife laws. Ratitae and their products are farm products and livestock for purposes of financial transactions and collateral.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.454.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Raising Ratitae is an agricultural pursuit.</span></h2>
          <p>Raising Ratitae is agricultural production and an agricultural pursuit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.454.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Sales of Ratitae and meat products.</span></h2>
          <p>Persons selling or buying Ratitae sold as livestock, sold for human consumption, or sold for slaughter must comply with chapters 17A, 28A, 31, 31A, and 31B.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.454.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Slaughter.</span></h2>
          <p>Ratitae must be slaughtered and inspected in accordance with the United States Department of Agriculture voluntary inspection program for exotic animals, Code of Federal Regulations, title 9, part 352.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.454.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Disease inspection.</span></h2>
          <p>Ratitae are subject to chapter 35 and the rules of the Board of Animal Health in the same manner as livestock and domestic animals, including provisions relating to importation and transportation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.454.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.9.4.0">1993 c 375 art 9 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LLAMA</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.455">
        <h1 class="shn">17.455 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.455.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.456">17.456</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.455.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Llama.</span></h2>
          <p>"Llama" means a member of the genus lama that is raised for the purpose of producing fiber, meat, or animal by-products or as breeding stock.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.455.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Owner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Owner" means a person who owns or is responsible for the raising of llamas.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.455.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.9.5.0">1993 c 375 art 9 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.456">
        <h1 class="shn">17.456 LLAMA.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.456.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Llamas are livestock.</span></h2>
          <p>Llamas are livestock and are not wild animals for purposes of hunting or wildlife laws. Llamas and their products are farm products and livestock for purposes of financial transactions and collateral.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.456.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Raising llamas is an agricultural pursuit.</span></h2>
          <p>Raising llamas is agricultural production and an agricultural pursuit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.456.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Sales of llamas and meat products.</span></h2>
          <p>Persons selling or buying llamas sold as livestock, sold for human consumption, or sold for slaughter must comply with chapters 17A, 28A, 31, 31A, and 31B.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.456.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/220/#laws.0.5.0">1997 c 220 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.456.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Disease inspection.</span></h2>
          <p>Llamas are subject to chapter 35 and the rules of the Board of Animal Health in the same manner as livestock and domestic animals, including provisions relating to importation and transportation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.456.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.9.6.0">1993 c 375 art 9 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">EURASIAN WILD PIGS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.457">
        <h1 class="shn">17.457 RESTRICTED SPECIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.457.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p>
          <p>(b) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of agriculture or the commissioner's designee.</p>
          <p>(c) "Domestic hogs" means members of the subspecies <i>Sus scrofa domesticus</i>.</p>
          <p>(d) "Restricted species" means pigs, boars, peccaries, and all other members of the Suidae family and the Tayassuidae family, excluding domestic hogs.</p>
          <p>(e) "Release" means an intentional introduction or persistent accidental escape of a restricted species or domestic hog from the control of the owner or responsible party. Release does not mean an accidental escape of restricted species or domestic hogs due to a transportation accident or an act of God.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.457.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote"> Restricted species permit required.</span></h2>
          <p>It is unlawful for a person to import, possess, propagate, or transport a restricted species, unless the person has a permit as described in subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.457.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Release of restricted species or domestic hogs prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) It is unlawful for a person to release restricted species or domestic hogs.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the penalties in subdivision 6, a person who violates paragraph (a) must do the following at the person's expense and by the date and time specified by the commissioner:</p>
          <p>(1) register their premises with the Board of Animal Health;</p>
          <p>(2) implement the confinement standards and record-keeping requirements developed by the Board of Animal Health; and</p>
          <p>(3) reimburse the commissioner for costs incurred to annually inspect the registered premises and verify compliance with clause (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.457.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Permits.</span></h2>
          <p> The commissioner may issue permits for the transportation, possession, purchase, or importation of restricted species only for scientific, research, or educational purposes. A permit issued under this subdivision may be revoked by the commissioner if the conditions of the permit are not met by the permittee or for any unlawful act or omission, including accidental escapes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.457.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Notice of release of restricted species or domestic hogs.</span></h2>
          <p>In the event of a release of a restricted species or domestic hog, the owner must notify within 24 hours a conservation officer and the Board of Animal Health and is responsible for the recovery of the species. If the owner does not provide notification or fails to recover the animal within 72 hours of providing notification, the released animal is considered feral swine under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.56">97A.56</a>, is no longer the personal property of the owner, and may be captured or destroyed at the former owner's expense by a peace officer or by the commissioner of natural resources under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.045#stat.97A.045.1">97A.045, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b), or other authority.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.457.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This section may be enforced by a peace officer, an enforcement officer under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.205">97A.205</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.211">97A.211,</a> and, except as provided in paragraph (b), by the commissioner under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.982">17.982</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.983">17.983</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) For the first violation of this section, the commissioner may impose an administrative penalty of no more than $1,000. For a second violation, the commissioner may impose an administrative penalty of no more than $1,500. For a third or succeeding violation, the commissioner may impose an administrative penalty of no more than $3,000 for each violation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.457.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person who violates subdivision 2, 2a, 4, or 7 is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
          <p>(b) A person who violates subdivision 2a, paragraph (a), is liable to the state for costs associated with a release. The attorney general may enforce this paragraph on behalf of any state agency affected.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.457.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote"> Identification requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>A restricted species in the possession of a person must be marked in a permanent fashion to identify ownership. The restricted species must be marked as soon as practicable after birth or purchase.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.457.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Containment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner, in consultation with the commissioner of natural resources, shall develop criteria for approved containment measures for restricted species.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.457.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Bond; security.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who possesses restricted species must provide proof of insurance or file a security bond with the commissioner in an amount determined by the commissioner to pay for the potential costs and damages that would be caused by the release of a restricted species.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.457.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Fee.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may impose a fee for permits in an amount sufficient to cover the costs of issuing the permits and for facility inspections. The fee may not exceed $50. Fee receipts must be deposited in the general fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.457.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/129/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 129 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/623/#laws.1.16.0">1994 c 623 art 1 s 16</a>-18,46; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.30.0">1999 c 231 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.26.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/43/#laws.2.11.0">2023 c 43 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/85/#laws.0.8.0">2024 c 85 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.6.1.0">2024 c 116 art 6 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">AGROFORESTRY</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.458">
        <h1 class="shn">17.458 AGROFORESTRY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.458.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agroforestry" means the cultivation of short-rotation woody crops using agricultural practices to produce timber or forest products.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.458.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agricultural pursuit.</span></h2>
          <p>Agroforestry is an agricultural pursuit.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.458.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.23.0">1997 c 216 s 23</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">HORSE FARMING</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.459">
        <h1 class="shn">17.459 HORSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.459.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Classification as livestock.</span></h2>
          <p>Horses and other equines raised for the purposes of riding, driving, farm or ranch work, competition, racing, recreation, sale, or as breeding stock are livestock. Horses and their products are livestock and farm products for purposes of financial transactions and collateral.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.459.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agricultural pursuit.</span></h2>
          <p>Raising horses and other equines is agricultural production and an agricultural pursuit. A horse breeding farm, horse training farm, horse boarding farm, or a farm combining those purposes is an agricultural operation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.459.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/203/#laws.0.3.0">2012 c 203 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.459.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/333/#laws.1.5.0">2010 c 333 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/203/#laws.0.2.0">2012 c 203 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">AQUACULTURE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.46">
        <h1 class="shn">17.46 SHORT TITLE.</h1>
        <p>Laws 1991, chapter 309, sections 4 to 16, may be cited as the Aquaculture Development Act.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.46.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/309/#laws.0.3.0">1991 c 309 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.47">
        <h1 class="shn">17.47 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.47.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to Laws 1991, chapter 309, sections 2 to 16.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.47.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Aquaculture.</span></h2>
          <p>"Aquaculture" means the culture of private aquatic life for consumption or sale.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.47.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Aquatic farm.</span></h2>
          <p>"Aquatic farm" means a facility used for the purpose of culturing private aquatic life in waters, including but not limited to artificial ponds, vats, tanks, raceways, other indoor or outdoor facilities that an aquatic farmer owns or where an aquatic farmer has exclusive control of, or private fish hatcheries licensed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97C.211">97C.211</a> for the sole purpose of processing or cultivating aquatic life.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.47.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Aquatic farmer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Aquatic farmer" means an individual who practices aquaculture.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.47.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.47.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Department.</span></h2>
          <p>"Department" means the Department of Agriculture.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.47.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Private aquatic life.</span></h2>
          <p>"Private aquatic life" means fish, shellfish, mollusks, crustaceans, turtles, and any other aquatic animals cultured within an aquatic farm. Private aquatic life is the property of the aquatic farmer.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.47.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/309/#laws.0.4.0">1991 c 309 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/465/#laws.2.3.0">1994 c 465 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/351/#laws.0.1.0">2002 c 351 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.49">
        <h1 class="shn">17.49 AQUACULTURE PROGRAM AND PROMOTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.49.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.58.0">2001 c 161 s 58</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.49.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Coordination.</span></h2>
          <p>Aquaculture programs in the state must be coordinated through the commissioner of agriculture. The commissioner of agriculture shall direct the development of aquaculture in the state. Aquaculture research, projects, and demonstrations must be reported to the commissioner before state appropriations for the research, projects, and demonstrations are encumbered. The commissioner shall maintain a database of aquaculture research, demonstrations, and other related information pertaining to aquaculture in the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.49.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Development program.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may establish a Minnesota aquaculture development and aid program that may support applied research, demonstration, financing, marketing, promotion, broodstock development, and other services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.49.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/94/#laws.1.107.0">2009 c 94 art 1 s 107</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.49.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/186/#laws.0.15.0">1987 c 186 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.26.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 26</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/318/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 318 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/350/#laws.4.1.0">1989 c 350 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/502/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 502 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/309/#laws.0.5.0">1991 c 309 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/345/#laws.2.11.0">1991 c 345 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/163/#laws.1.1.0">1993 c 163 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/192/#laws.0.12.0">1997 c 192 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.491">
        <h1 class="shn">17.491 AQUACULTURE IS AGRICULTURAL PURSUIT.</h1>
        <p>Aquaculture is an agricultural pursuit.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.491.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/350/#laws.4.2.0">1989 c 350 art 4 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.492"><b>17.492</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/309/#laws.0.17.0">1991 c 309 s 17</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.494">
        <h1 class="shn">17.494 AQUACULTURE PERMITS; RULES.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner shall act as permit or license coordinator for aquatic farmers and shall assist aquatic farmers to obtain licenses or permits.</p>
        <p>(b) A state agency issuing multiple permits or licenses for aquaculture shall consolidate the permits or licenses required for every aquatic farm location. The Department of Natural Resources transportation permits are exempt from this requirement. State agencies shall adopt rules or issue commissioner's orders that establish permit and license requirements, approval timelines, and compliance standards. Saltwater aquatic farms, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4982">17.4982</a>, and processing facilities for saltwater aquatic life, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4982">17.4982</a>, must be classified as agricultural operations for purposes of any construction, discharge, or other permit issued by the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        <p>Nothing in this section modifies any state agency's regulatory authority over aquaculture production.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.494.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/309/#laws.0.6.0">1991 c 309 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/38/#laws.0.1.0">2019 c 38 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.495">
        <h1 class="shn">17.495 APPEAL PROCEDURES.</h1>
        <p>A state agency that denies a license or permit to an aquatic farmer shall provide the aquatic farmer with a written notice specifying the reasons for refusal.</p>
        <p>An aquatic farmer may appeal a state agency's denial of the license or permit in a contested case proceeding under chapter 14.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.495.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/309/#laws.0.7.0">1991 c 309 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.496">
        <h1 class="shn">17.496 QUARANTINE FACILITY; RULES.</h1>
        <p>By July 1, 1992, the commissioner of natural resources shall adopt rules, in consultation with the commissioner of agriculture and the Aquaculture Advisory Committee, for the construction and operation of a quarantine facility for fish eggs presently requiring quarantine and disposition of fish from the facility. Fish in a quarantine station that are determined to be disease-free under the procedures developed by the commissioner of natural resources may be bought, sold, or transported.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.496.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/309/#laws.0.8.0">1991 c 309 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.497">
        <h1 class="shn">17.497 EXOTIC SPECIES IMPORTATION; RULES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of natural resources shall establish rules, in consultation with the commissioner of agriculture and the Aquaculture Advisory Committee, for approving or rejecting importation of "exotic" or genetically altered aquatic species to protect the integrity of the natural ecosystem and provide aquatic farmers with information that may affect business decisions.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.497.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/309/#laws.0.9.0">1991 c 309 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.498">
        <h1 class="shn">17.498 RULES; FINANCIAL ASSURANCE.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency, after consultation and cooperation with the commissioners of agriculture and natural resources, shall present proposed rules to the Pollution Control Agency board prescribing water quality permit requirements for aquaculture facilities by May 1, 1992. The rules must consider:</p>
        <p>(1) best available proven technology, best management practices, and water treatment practices that prevent and minimize degradation of waters of the state considering economic factors, availability, technical feasibility, effectiveness, and environmental impacts;</p>
        <p>(2) classes, types, sizes, and categories of aquaculture facilities;</p>
        <p>(3) temporary reversible impacts versus long-term impacts on water quality;</p>
        <p>(4) effects on drinking water supplies that cause adverse human health concerns; and</p>
        <p>(5) aquaculture therapeutics, which shall be regulated by the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        <p>(b) Net pen aquaculture and other aquaculture facilities with similar effects must submit an annual report to the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency analyzing changes in water quality trends from previous years, documentation of best management practices, documentation of costs to restore the waters used for aquaculture to the trophic state existing before aquatic farming was initiated, and documentation of financial assurance in an amount adequate to pay for restoration costs. The trophic state, which is the productivity of the waters measured by total phosphorus, dissolved oxygen, algae abundance as chlorophyll-a, and secchi disk depth of light penetration, and the condition of the waters measured by raw drinking water parameters, shall be determined to the extent possible before aquatic farming is initiated. The financial assurance may be a trust fund, letter of credit, escrow account, surety bond, or other financial assurance payable to the commissioner for restoration of the waters if the permittee cannot or will not restore the waters after termination of aquatic farming operations or revocation of the permit.</p>
        <p>(c) Information received as part of a permit application or as otherwise requested must be classified according to chapter 13. Information about processes, aquatic farming procedures, feed and therapeutic formulas and rates, and tests on aquatic farming products that have economic value is nonpublic data under chapter 13, if requested by the applicant or permittee.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.498.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/309/#laws.0.10.0">1991 c 309 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/86/#laws.3.4.0">1999 c 86 art 3 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.4981">
        <h1 class="shn">17.4981 GENERAL CONDITIONS FOR REGULATION OF AQUATIC FARMS.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) Aquatic farms are licensed to culture private aquatic life. Cultured aquatic life is not wildlife. Aquatic farms must be licensed and given classifications to prevent or minimize impacts on natural resources. The purpose of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4981">17.4981</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4997">17.4997</a> is to:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) prevent public aquatic life from entering an aquatic farm;</p>
        <p class="in">(2) prevent release of nonindigenous or exotic species into public waters without approval of the commissioner;</p>
        <p class="in">(3) protect against release of disease pathogens to public waters;</p>
        <p class="in">(4) protect existing natural aquatic habitats and the wildlife dependent on them; and</p>
        <p class="in">(5) protect private aquatic life from unauthorized taking or harvest.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) Private aquatic life that is legally acquired and possessed is an article of interstate commerce and may be restricted only as necessary to protect state fish and water resources.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) The commissioner of natural resources shall establish license and other fees as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1285#stat.16A.1285.2">16A.1285, subdivision 2</a>, that would make aquaculture licensing and enforcement self-sustaining. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1283">16A.1283</a>, the commissioner may, by written order published in the State Register, establish the fees required by this section. The fees are not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply. The commissioner shall develop best management practices for aquaculture to ensure the long-term sustainability of aquaculture and wetlands used for aquaculture, including, but not limited to, fish farming in man-made ponds.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.4981.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/566/#laws.0.1.0">1992 c 566 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.2.2.0">2008 c 368 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.2.2.0">2009 c 176 art 2 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.4982">
        <h1 class="shn">17.4982 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4981">17.4981</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4998">17.4998</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Approved laboratory methods.</span></h2>
          <p>"Approved laboratory methods" means methods described in the latest edition of the "Procedures for the Detection and Identification of Certain Fish Pathogens" published by the American Fisheries Society Fish Health Section known as the Fish Health Blue Book.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Aquaculture therapeutics.</span></h2>
          <p>"Aquaculture therapeutics" means drugs, medications, and disease control chemicals that are approved for aquaculture use by the United States Food and Drug Administration or the United States Environmental Protection Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Aquarium facilities.</span></h2>
          <p>"Aquarium facilities" means facilities that rear or hold private aquatic life for sale for aquarium or display purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Aquatic farm.</span></h2>
          <p>"Aquatic farm" means a licensed facility used for hatching, raising, rearing, and culturing private aquatic life in waters and preparing aquatic life for sale, including, but not limited to, ponds, vats, tanks, raceways, and other indoor or outdoor facilities that an aquatic farmer owns or waters of which an aquatic farmer has the use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Aquatic life.</span></h2>
          <p>"Aquatic life" has the meaning given to "private aquatic life" in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.47#stat.17.47.7">17.47, subdivision 7</a>, and for purposes of commercial transactions, aquatic life is livestock.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Certifiable diseases.</span></h2>
          <p>"Certifiable diseases" includes any of the following expressed as clinical symptoms or based on the presence of the pathogen: channel catfish virus, <i>Renibacterium salmoninarum</i> (bacterial kidney disease), <i>Aeromonas salmonicida </i>(bacterial furunculosis), <i>Yersinia ruckeri</i> (enteric redmouth disease), <i>Edwardsiella ictaluri </i>(enteric septicemia of catfish), infectious hematopoietic necrosis virus, infectious pancreatic necrosis virus, <i>Myxobolus cerebralis</i> (whirling disease), <i>Tetracapsuloides bryosalmonae </i>(proliferative kidney disease), viral hemorrhagic septicemia virus, epizootic epitheliotropic virus, <i>Ceratomyxa shasta</i> (ceratomyxosis), and any emergency fish disease.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of natural resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Containment facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Containment facility" means a licensed facility for salmonids, catfish, or species on the VHS-susceptible-species list that complies with clauses (1), (3), and (4), or clauses (2), (3), and (4):</p>
          <p>(1) disinfects its effluent to the standards in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4991">17.4991</a> before the effluent is discharged to public waters;</p>
          <p>(2) does not discharge to public waters or to waters of the state directly connected to public waters;</p>
          <p>(3) raises aquatic life that is prohibited from being released into the wild and must be kept in a facility approved by the commissioner unless processed for food consumption;</p>
          <p>(4) contains aquatic life requiring a fish health inspection prior to transportation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.8a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8a.<span class="headnote">Emergency enzootic disease area.</span></h2>
          <p>"Emergency enzootic disease area" means an enzootic disease area that harbors an emergency fish disease. Trout, salmon, or catfish species are from an emergency enzootic disease area only if the individual species in question can carry one or more of the emergency fish disease pathogens present.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Emergency fish disease.</span></h2>
          <p>"Emergency fish disease" means designated fish diseases or pathogens not already present in this state that could impact populations of aquatic life if inadvertently released by infected aquatic life, including channel catfish virus, viral hemorrhagic septicemia virus, infectious hematopoietic necrosis virus, infectious pancreatic necrosis virus, whirling disease, ceratomyxosis, proliferative kidney disease, and epizootic epitheliotropic virus disease.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Enzootic disease area.</span></h2>
          <p>"Enzootic disease area" means an area with well-defined geographic boundaries which harbors one or more certifiable diseases pathogens.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.10a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10a.<span class="headnote">Fish collector.</span></h2>
          <p>"Fish collector" means an individual who has been certified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4989">17.4989</a> to oversee the collection of fish samples from a facility or a water body for disease testing by a certified laboratory.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Fish Health Blue Book.</span></h2>
          <p>"Fish Health Blue Book" means the standardized set of procedures and guidelines established and published by the American Fisheries Society Fish Health Section for the detection and isolation of fish pathogens.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Fish health inspection.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Fish health inspection" means an on-site, statistically based sampling, collection, and testing of fish in accordance with processes in the Fish Health Blue Book or the Diagnostic Manual for Aquatic Animal Diseases, published by the International Office of Epizootics (OIE) to test for causative pathogens. The samples for inspection must be collected by a fish health inspector or a fish collector in cooperation with the producer. Testing of samples must be done by an approved laboratory.</p>
          <p>(b) The inspection for viral hemorrhagic septicemia (VHS), infectious pancreatic necrosis (IPN), and infectious hematopoietic necrosis (IHN) in salmonids and for VHS in nonsalmonids must include at a minimum viral testing of ovarian fluids at the 95 percent confidence level of detecting two percent incidence of disease.</p>
          <p>(c) The inspection for certifiable diseases and pathogens for wild fish must follow the guidelines of the Fish Health Blue Book or the Diagnostic Manual for Aquatic Animal Diseases.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Fish health inspector.</span></h2>
          <p>"Fish health inspector" means an individual certified as a fish health inspector or an aquatic animal health inspector by the American Fisheries Society or state, federal, or provincial resource management agency, except that a certification may not be made by an inspector who has a conflict of interest in connection with the outcome of the certification.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Game fish.</span></h2>
          <p>"Game fish" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.015#stat.97A.015.25">97A.015, subdivision 25</a>, except that green or orange spotted sunfish are not game fish for purposes of determining fish of significant public value.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Intensive culture.</span></h2>
          <p>"Intensive culture" means the rearing of fish at densities greater than can be supported in the natural environment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Licensed facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Licensed facility" means a licensed aquatic farm, including all licensed waters.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Lot.</span></h2>
          <p>"Lot" means a group of fish of the same species and age that originated from the same discrete spawning population and that always have shared a common water supply, or various age groups of adult brood stock of the same species that have shared the same containers for one brood cycle.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Minnows.</span></h2>
          <p>"Minnows" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.015#stat.97A.015.29">97A.015, subdivision 29</a>, except the 12-inch restriction on sucker minnows does not apply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.18a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18a.<span class="headnote">Nonindigenous species.</span></h2>
          <p>"Nonindigenous species" means a species of fish or other aquatic life that is:</p>
          <p>(1) not known to have been historically present in the state;</p>
          <p>(2) not known to be naturally occurring in a particular part of the state; or</p>
          <p>(3) designated by rule as a prohibited or regulated invasive species.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.18b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18b.<span class="headnote">Nonindigenous strain.</span></h2>
          <p>"Nonindigenous strain" means a species of fish or other aquatic life that:</p>
          <p>(1) has an original source outside of this state and contiguous states;</p>
          <p>(2) is an unnaturally occurring hybrid or genetically engineered species; or</p>
          <p>(3) in areas north of marked State Highway 210, is a walleye, the original source of which is from south of marked State Highway 210 or from outside the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.18c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18c.<span class="headnote">Processing.</span></h2>
          <p>"Processing" means rendering a species of aquatic life for food, bait, or other purposes so that it is no longer alive.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.<span class="headnote">Public waters.</span></h2>
          <p>"Public waters" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.005#stat.103G.005.15">103G.005, subdivision 15</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.20">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20.<span class="headnote">Quarantine facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Quarantine facility" means a culture system that is enclosed in a building and is separated from other fish culture facilities where fish can be isolated and maintained while preventing their introduction and pathogen introduction into the environment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.20a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20a.<span class="headnote">Saltwater aquaculture.</span></h2>
          <p>"Saltwater aquaculture" means the commercial propagation and rearing of saltwater aquatic life primarily for consumption as human food.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.20b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20b.<span class="headnote">Saltwater aquatic farm.</span></h2>
          <p>"Saltwater aquatic farm" means a facility used for saltwater aquaculture including but not limited to artificial ponds, vats, tanks, raceways, and other facilities that an aquatic farmer owns or has exclusive control of for the sole purpose of producing saltwater aquatic life.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.20c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20c.<span class="headnote">Saltwater aquatic life.</span></h2>
          <p>"Saltwater aquatic life" means aquatic species that are saltwater obligates or perform optimally when raised in salinities closer to that of natural seawater and need saltwater to survive. Saltwater aquatic life includes but is not limited to crustaceans.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.21">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21.<span class="headnote">Standard facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Standard facility" means a licensed facility that is not a quarantine or containment facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.21a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21a.<span class="headnote">VHS-susceptible species.</span></h2>
          <p>"VHS-susceptible species" are aquatic species that are natural hosts for viral hemorrhagic septicemia according to the Fish Health Blue Book or the book's successor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.21b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21b.<span class="headnote">VHS-susceptible-species list.</span></h2>
          <p>"VHS-susceptible-species list" is the VHS-susceptible species listed in the Fish Health Blue Book that are found in or that can survive in the Great Lakes region.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4982.22">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22.<span class="headnote">Waters of the state.</span></h2>
          <p>"Waters of the state" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.005#stat.103G.005.17">103G.005, subdivision 17</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.4982.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/566/#laws.0.2.0">1992 c 566 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/226/#laws.0.1.0">1993 c 226 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.3.0">1996 c 410 s 3</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/226/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 226 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/243/#laws.0.1.0">2004 c 243 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.3.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 3</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/289/#laws.0.1.0">2014 c 289 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.3.7.0">2016 c 189 art 3 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/38/#laws.0.2.0">2019 c 38 s 2</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.2.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 2</a>-7</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.4983">
        <h1 class="shn">17.4983 AQUATIC FARM OPERATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4983.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Acquisition and sale of private aquatic life.</span></h2>
          <p>Aquatic life legally possessed may be bought, acquired, and sold by licensed facilities as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4981">17.4981</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4997">17.4997</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4983.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Acquisition from state.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may sell aquatic life to licensed facilities at fair wholesale market value. Fair wholesale market value must be determined by the average market price charged in this state and contiguous states and provinces for similar quantities.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall establish procedures to make aquatic life available to licensed facilities if state aquatic life would otherwise die or go to waste, such as in cases of winterkill lakes, waters where piscicides will be applied, and waters subject to extreme draw-down. The public must be given angling opportunities if public access is available.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall attempt to provide opportunities to make brood stock available to licensed facilities to reduce reliance on out-of-state sources without causing adverse impacts to game fish and native rough fish populations.</p>
          <p>(d) If the commissioner denies approval to obtain aquatic life outside the state, a written notice must be submitted to the applicant stating the reasons for denial, and the commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) designate approved sources if available to obtain the desired aquatic life; or</p>
          <p>(2) sell the aquatic life from state hatcheries at fair wholesale market value if there is a surplus from state operations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4983.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Methods to harvest aquatic life.</span></h2>
          <p>Licensed facilities may use all reasonable methods to operate and harvest aquatic life from licensed facilities, including available nets.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4983.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Discharge may require permit.</span></h2>
          <p>The discharge from an aquatic farm must comply with discharge permits required by the Minnesota Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4983.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Ownership of aquatic life.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding other provisions of law, aquatic life lawfully acquired and possessed by a licensed facility is private aquatic life and property of the owner of the licensed facility.</p>
          <p>(b) The state may not seize or otherwise confiscate private aquatic life without due process of law, except that private aquatic life in public waters may become property of the state if the waters are not part of a licensed facility. The commissioner shall notify the licensee that the aquatic life in a facility that is no longer licensed will become property of the state if the aquatic life is not removed. If the licensee does not respond in writing within 30 days after receiving the notice and make alternative arrangements, or does not remove the aquatic life by 60 ice-free days after receiving the notice, the private aquatic life becomes property of the state.</p>
          <p>(c) Private aquatic life that is transferred to the state or released into public waters that are not part of a licensed facility is owned by the state and may be considered wildlife.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4983.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Control of licensed waters.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the public cannot legally access waters of the state that are part of a licensed aquatic farm except by permission of the licensee, the use of the waters by the public is subject to restriction by the licensee.</p>
          <p>(b) Waters of the state may not be licensed for aquaculture use to more than one licensee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4983.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Angling in licensed waters.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not take fish by angling from waters subject to subdivision 6 unless the person has written permission from the licensee and:</p>
          <p>(1) has an invoice when in possession of fish; or</p>
          <p>(2) takes fish under an angling license, subject to the limits and conditions in the game and fish laws.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4983.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Interference prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not knowingly damage, disturb, or interfere with legal aquatic farm operations.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.4983.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/566/#laws.0.3.0">1992 c 566 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/226/#laws.0.4.0">1993 c 226 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/226/#laws.0.4.0">1997 c 226 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.2.1.0">2024 c 90 art 2 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.4984">
        <h1 class="shn">17.4984 AQUATIC FARM LICENSE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4984.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License required.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A person or entity may not operate an aquatic farm without first obtaining an aquatic farm license from the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Applications for an aquatic farm license must be made on forms provided by the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Licenses are valid for five years and are transferable upon notification to the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The commissioner shall issue an aquatic farm license on payment of the required license fee under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4988">17.4988</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) A license issued by the commissioner is not a determination of private property rights, but is only based on a determination that the licensee does not have a significant detrimental impact on the public resource.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) The commissioner shall not issue a new license for aquatic farm purposes on a natural water body that has been restored or subject to a protective easement or other interest in land that was at least partially paid for with state or federal money.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) Before a new aquatic farm license is issued for a natural water body, the applicant must notify all owners of property with direct access to the water body. The notification must include the language of this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4984.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Listed waters.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An aquatic farm license must list:</p>
          <p>(1) the specific waters of the state that may be used in connection with the licensed aquatic farm and the species approved for each licensed water; and</p>
          <p>(2) whether aeration requiring a permit is approved.</p>
          <p class="bl">Additional waters may not be used until they are approved by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The right to use waters licensed for private fish hatchery or aquatic farm purposes may be transferred between licensees with prior approval by the commissioner if requirements for species to be raised are met. Waters that are continually connected by a permanent watercourse to other waters must not be approved for aquatic farm use, except that connected waters that are isolated from other waters may be licensed as a single water body. Waters that are intermittently connected or may become connected with other waters may be denied, or screening or other measures may be required to prevent passage of aquatic life. Listed waters may be changed on approval by the area fisheries supervisor or the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall conduct an inspection of waters to be licensed prior to approving or denying initial licensing of the waters. When artificial tanks, jars, or other containers are added to existing licensed facilities, an additional inspection is not required.</p>
          <p>(d) Waters containing fish of significant public value, including game fish and native rough fish, may be denied licensing unless the applicant can demonstrate exclusive riparian control.</p>
          <p>(e) Waters containing fish of significant public value, including game fish and native rough fish, may be denied licensing unless the game fish and native rough fish of significant public value are, at the commissioner's option, and taking into consideration the recommendation of the licensed applicant, sold to the licensee, or removed by the Department of Natural Resources or disposed of as provided in writing by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(f) Waters licensed under an aquatic farm license may be aerated during open water periods without a separate aeration permit.</p>
          <p>(g) Common carp and bullheads may be removed from licensed waters, and transported and disposed of by the licensee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4984.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Listed species.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An aquatic farm license must list the species of aquatic life appropriate for the classification of the waters. Listed species of aquatic life may be changed on written request to and approval by the area fisheries supervisor. Species of aquatic life regulated under chapter 97A, 97B, or 97C may not be cultured unless listed on the license.</p>
          <p>(b) All waters licensed before July 1, 1992, under a private fish farm or fish hatchery license must be approved for species listed under current licenses if other conditions for licensing are met.</p>
          <p>(c) If licensed waters are located within a 25-year floodplain and are not enclosed within a building, species of aquatic life may be licensed at the discretion of the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(d) Licensed waters located outside of a 25-year floodplain or enclosed within a building may be licensed for any species, except that the commissioner may deny licensing for species not present in the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4984.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Single license for aquatic farming operation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall issue a single license for aquatic farming, with the following information and endorsements:</p>
          <p>(1) waters covered by the license;</p>
          <p>(2) classification of each of the licensed waters;</p>
          <p>(3) aeration endorsement for each licensed water where the licensee has exclusive control of riparian access or where the conditions for an aeration permit have been met; and</p>
          <p>(4) endorsements requested by the licensee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4984.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">State list of waters.</span></h2>
          <p>If the state uses waters of the state for aquatic farming, the state shall acquire legal access to the waters and make documentation of the access available to the public.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4984.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Inspections and enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The premises, property, vehicles, private aquatic life, and equipment where private aquatic farm operations are being conducted are subject to reasonable and necessary inspections at reasonable times by conservation officers. The reason for the inspection must be provided in writing. The owner, operator, or designee may be present when inspections are conducted.</p>
          <p>(b) Conservation officers may enforce sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4981">17.4981</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4997">17.4997</a> under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.205">97A.205</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4984.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Nonpublic records.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Licensees must keep complete, up-to-date, nonpublic records of the operation of the aquatic farm. The records must remain available for at least three years.</p>
          <p>(b) The records must be in English and include the following information:</p>
          <p>(1) for each species acquired, the number or pounds of fish or eggs acquired, names and addresses of the sources from which acquired, and the dates of receipt;</p>
          <p>(2) for each species sold or disposed of, the number or pounds of fish sold or disposed of, the names and addresses of the purchasers or persons to whom the conveyances are made, and the dates of sale; and</p>
          <p>(3) for fish sperm or viable eggs, the amount acquired or sold, the names and addresses of the sources from which acquired, the purchasers to whom conveyed, and the dates of purchase or sale.</p>
          <p>(c) On or before March 1 of each year, the licensee shall submit a complete annual report on a form furnished by the commissioner, covering the number or pounds of all species sold or purchased in the preceding licensed year.</p>
          <p>(d) An aquatic farmer shall maintain records for reasonable inspection by the commissioner. Information on aquatic life production, harvest, and sales is nonpublic information.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.4984.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/566/#laws.0.4.0">1992 c 566 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/226/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 226 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.8.0">1996 c 410 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/331/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 331 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.14.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.2.3.0">2008 c 368 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.2.2.0">2024 c 90 art 2 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.4985">
        <h1 class="shn">17.4985 TRANSPORTATION OF AQUATIC LIFE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4985.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requirements for importation, transportation within the state, or stocking of fish.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivision 3, a licensee may not import aquatic life into the state, transport aquatic life within the state, or stock waters of the state with aquatic life without first obtaining a bill of lading or transportation permit from the commissioner, with disease certification, if applicable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4985.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Bill of lading.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A state-issued bill of lading is required for:</p>
          <p>(1) intrastate transportation of aquatic life other than salmonids, catfish, or species on the VHS-susceptible-species list between licensed private fish hatcheries, aquatic farms, or aquarium facilities licensed for the species being transported if the aquatic life is being transported into a watershed where it is not currently present, if walleyes whose original source is south of marked State Highway 210 are being transported to a facility north of marked State Highway 210, or if the original source of the aquatic life is outside Minnesota and contiguous states; and</p>
          <p>(2) stocking waters other than public waters with aquatic life other than salmonids, catfish, or species on the VHS-susceptible-species list.</p>
          <p>(b) When aquatic life is transported under paragraph (a), a copy of the bill of lading must be submitted to the regional fisheries manager at least 72 hours before the transportation.</p>
          <p>(c) For transportation and stocking of waters that are not public waters:</p>
          <p>(1) a bill of lading must be submitted to the regional fisheries manager 72 hours before transporting fish for stocking;</p>
          <p>(2) a bill of lading must be submitted to the regional fisheries manager within five days after stocking if the waters to be stocked are confirmed by telecopy or telephone prior to stocking by the regional fisheries office not to be public waters; or</p>
          <p>(3) a completed bill of lading may be submitted to the regional fisheries office by telecopy prior to transporting fish for stocking. Confirmation that the waters to be stocked are not public waters may be made by returning the bill of lading by telecopy or in writing, in which cases additional copies need not be submitted to the Department of Natural Resources.</p>
          <p>(d) Bill of lading forms may only be issued by the Department of Natural Resources in St. Paul, and new bill of lading forms may not be issued until all previously issued forms have been returned.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4985.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Exemptions for transportation permits and bills of lading.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A state-issued bill of lading or transportation permit is not required by an aquatic farm licensee for importing animals not on the VHS-susceptible-species list, transporting animals not on the VHS-susceptible-species list, or exporting the following:</p>
          <p>(1) minnows taken under an aquatic farm license in this state and transported intrastate;</p>
          <p>(2) aquarium or ornamental fish including goldfish and tropical, subtropical, and saltwater species that cannot survive in the waters of the state, which may be imported or transported if accompanied by shipping documents;</p>
          <p>(3) fish or fish eggs that have been processed for use as food, bait, or other purposes unrelated to fish propagation;</p>
          <p>(4) live fish from a licensed aquatic farm, which may be transported directly to an outlet for processing or for other food purposes if accompanied by shipping documents;</p>
          <p>(5) fish being exported if accompanied by shipping documents;</p>
          <p>(6) sucker eggs, sucker fry, or fathead minnows transported intrastate for bait propagation or feeding of cultural aquatic life, except that if either species becomes listed on the VHS-susceptible-species list, then a transportation permit is required;</p>
          <p>(7) species of fish that are found within the state used in connection with public shows, exhibits, demonstrations, or fishing pools for periods not exceeding 14 days;</p>
          <p>(8) fish being transported through the state if accompanied by shipping documents; or</p>
          <p>(9) intrastate transportation of aquatic life between or within licensed private fish hatcheries, aquatic farms, or aquarium facilities licensed for the species being transported, except where required in subdivision 2 and except that salmonids, catfish, or species on the VHS-susceptible-species list may only be transferred or transported intrastate without a transportation permit if they had no record of bacterial kidney disease or viral hemorrhagic septicemia at the time they were imported into the state and if they have had a fish health inspection within the preceding year that has shown no certifiable diseases to be present.</p>
          <p>Aquatic life being transferred between licensed private fish hatcheries, aquatic farms, or aquarium facilities must be accompanied by shipping documents and salmonids, catfish, or species on the VHS-susceptible-species list being transferred or transported intrastate without a transportation permit must be accompanied by a copy of their most recent fish health inspection.</p>
          <p>(b) Shipping documents required under paragraph (a) must show the place of origin, owner or consignee, destination, number, and species.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4985.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Transportation permit requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>A transportation permit is required for all importation, transportation, or stocking of private aquatic life not covered by subdivision 2 or exempted in subdivision 3. A transportation permit may be used for multiple shipments within the 30-day term for the permit if the source and the destination remains the same. Transportation permits, which may authorize importation or stocking of public waters, may be issued through Department of Natural Resources regional offices or the St. Paul office, and must be obtained prior to shipment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4985.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Permit application.</span></h2>
          <p>An application for a transportation permit must be made on forms provided by the commissioner. An incomplete application must be rejected. An application for a transportation permit for salmonids, catfish, or species on the VHS-susceptible-species list; their eggs; or their sperm must be accompanied by certification that the source of the eggs or sperm are free of certifiable diseases, except that eggs with enteric redmouth, whirling disease, or furunculosis may be imported, transported, or stocked following treatment approved by the commissioner, and fish with bacterial kidney disease or viral hemorrhagic septicemia may be imported, transported, or stocked into areas where the disease has been identified as being present. A copy of the transportation permit showing the date of certification inspection must accompany the shipment of fish while in transit and must be available for inspection by the commissioner. By 14 days after a completed application is received, the commissioner must approve or deny the importation permits as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4985.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Vehicle identification.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A vehicle used by a licensee for transporting aquatic life must be identified with the licensee's name and town of residence as it appears on the license and the license number.</p>
          <p>(b) A vehicle used by a licensee must have identification displayed so that it is readily visible from either side of the vehicle in letters and numbers not less than 2-1/2 inches high and with a three-eighths inch wide stroke. Identification may be permanently affixed to vehicles or displayed on removable plates or placards placed on opposite doors of the vehicle or on the tanks carried on the vehicle.</p>
          <p>(c) An application to license a vehicle for minnow transport or export or for use as a fish vendor that is received by the commissioner is a temporary license until it is approved or denied.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.4985.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/566/#laws.0.5.0">1992 c 566 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/226/#laws.0.6.0">1993 c 226 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.10.0">1996 c 410 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/307/#laws.0.1.0">2008 c 307 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.8.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 8</a>-10</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.4986">
        <h1 class="shn">17.4986 IMPORTATION OF AQUATIC LIFE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4986.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Importation and stocking restrictions.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not import fish into or stock fish in the state without first obtaining a transportation permit with a disease certification, as prescribed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4985#stat.17.4985.5">17.4985, subdivision 5</a>, when required or a bill of lading from the commissioner, unless the person is exempted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4986.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Licensed facilities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall issue transportation permits to import:</p>
          <p>(1) indigenous and naturalized species except trout, salmon, catfish, or species on the VHS-susceptible-species list and sperm from any source to a standard facility;</p>
          <p>(2) trout, salmon, catfish, or species on the VHS-susceptible-species list from a nonemergency enzootic disease area to a containment facility if the fish are certified within the previous year to be free of certifiable diseases, except that eggs with enteric redmouth, whirling disease, or furunculosis may be imported following treatment approved by the commissioner, and fish with bacterial kidney disease or viral hemorrhagic septicemia may be imported into areas where the disease has been identified as being present; and</p>
          <p>(3) trout, salmon, catfish, or species on the VHS-susceptible-species list from a facility in a nonemergency enzootic disease area with a disease-free history of three years or more to a standard facility, except that eggs with enteric redmouth, whirling disease, or furunculosis may be imported following treatment approved by the commissioner, and fish with bacterial kidney disease or viral hemorrhagic septicemia may be imported into areas where the disease has been identified as being present.</p>
          <p>(b) If a source facility in a nonemergency enzootic disease area cannot demonstrate a history free from disease, aquatic life may only be imported into a quarantine facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4986.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Emergency enzootic disease area.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as otherwise provided and except that eggs with enteric redmouth, whirling disease, or furunculosis may be imported following treatment approved by the commissioner, and fish with bacterial kidney disease may be imported into areas where the disease has been previously introduced, fish may be imported from emergency enzootic disease areas only as fertilized eggs under the following conditions:</p>
          <p>(1) to be imported into a standard facility, fertilized eggs must have a disease-free history for at least five years;</p>
          <p>(2) to be imported into a containment facility, fertilized eggs must have a disease-free history for at least three years; or</p>
          <p>(3) to be imported into a quarantine facility, fertilized eggs may have a disease-free history of less than three years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.4986.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Disease-free history.</span></h2>
          <p>Disease-free histories required under this section must include the results of a fish health inspection. When disease-free histories of more than one year are required for importing salmonids, catfish, or species on the VHS-susceptible-species list, the disease history must be of consecutive years that include the year previous to, or the year of, the transportation request.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.4986.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/566/#laws.0.6.0">1992 c 566 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/226/#laws.0.8.0">1993 c 226 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.12.0">1996 c 410 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/307/#laws.0.4.0">2008 c 307 s 4</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/86/#laws.1.8.0">2009 c 86 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.11.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 11</a>,12</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.4987">
        <h1 class="shn">17.4987 STOCKING PRIVATE AQUATIC LIFE.</h1>
        <p>(a) A person may not release private aquatic life into public waters that are not licensed as part of an aquatic farm without first obtaining a transportation permit from the commissioner. The commissioner may:</p>
        <p>(1) deny issuance of a permit if releasing the private aquatic life is not consistent with the management plan for the public waters; and</p>
        <p>(2) approve the import, transport, and stocking of fish with bacterial kidney disease or viral hemorrhagic septicemia into areas or waters where either disease has been identified as being present.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner shall make management plans available to the public.</p>
        <p>(c) If a permit is denied, the commissioner must provide reasons for the denial in writing.</p>
      </div>
