        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.59.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Payment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish the procedure for the timely payment of the checkoff fee by the producer to the council. The procedure shall be clearly outlined in the proposed promotional order. The procedure must be fair, reasonable and the checkoff fee shall be deducted by the first purchaser at the time of sale. The first purchaser shall submit to the council any checkoff fees so deducted once every 30 days in accordance with the commissioner's rules.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.59.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Financing referendums.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall require producers petitioning for a promotional order to deposit in advance an amount necessary to defray the expense of electing the first council, formulating an order, submitting it to referendum and issuing the order. Funds received for that purpose shall be deposited in the commodity research and promotion account. If the order is issued, the producers shall be reimbursed when funds are available from assessments. If the order is not issued the commissioner shall refund only that portion of the deposit remaining after payment of expenses incurred on a pro rata basis.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.59.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Deposit and use of checkoff fees.</span></h2>
          <p>Checkoff fees collected pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.51">17.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.69">17.69</a> shall be deposited in a federally insured depository institution and shall be disbursed by the officers and employees approved by the council for the necessary expenses incurred in the administration of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.51">17.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.69">17.69</a>. Checkoff fees collected shall be used exclusively for the purpose collected and not to support or oppose a political party or a candidate for nomination or election to a public office.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.59.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Commodities research and promotion account.</span></h2>
          <p>All fees collected by the department under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.51">17.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.69">17.69</a> and any other fees and income received by the department in the administration of these statutes shall be deposited in a separate account known as the commodity research and promotion account in the agricultural fund. Money in the account, including interest, is appropriated to the commissioner to carry out the duties of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.51">17.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.69">17.69</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.59.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1021/#laws.0.9.0">1969 c 1021 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/149/#laws.0.62.0">1976 c 149 s 62</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/41/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 41 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.256.0">1981 c 356 s 256</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/582/#laws.0.6.0">1982 c 582 s 6</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.2.5.0">1987 c 384 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/350/#laws.5.1.0">1989 c 350 art 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/172/#laws.0.21.0">1993 c 172 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.31.0">1999 c 231 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.60">
        <h1 class="shn">17.60 COMPENSATION AND EXPENSES.</h1>
        <p>Each member of a council, except the commissioner, shall be entitled to a reasonable per diem, not exceeding the same rate of compensation per day as is authorized for payment to members of advisory councils and committees pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059#stat.15.059.3">15.059, subdivision 3</a>, while engaged in the performance of duties, and actual expenses incurred while attending council meetings or executive committee meetings. Payments to council members for other official business of the council require approval by the council.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.60.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1021/#laws.0.10.0">1969 c 1021 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/149/#laws.0.62.0">1976 c 149 s 62</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/582/#laws.0.9.0">1982 c 582 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.601"><b>17.601</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/582/#laws.0.14.0">1982 c 582 s 14</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.61">
        <h1 class="shn">17.61 LEGAL COUNSEL.</h1>
        <p>The council may appoint an attorney who shall act for the council and the commissioner when required. The council shall fix the compensation and terms of employment of such attorney. The provisions of chapter 8 shall not apply to this attorney.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.61.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1021/#laws.0.11.0">1969 c 1021 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/149/#laws.0.62.0">1976 c 149 s 62</a> subd 2</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.62">
        <h1 class="shn">17.62 COUNCIL RECORDS.</h1>
        <p>All of the records of a council, except as otherwise provided in this section, shall be open to the public and shall be available for inspection by any person for any lawful purpose, provided, however, that the council shall be empowered to make reasonable rules concerning the inspection of the records, the time or place of inspection, or the manner in which the information shall be made available. Financial information pertaining to individual participating producers shall not be open to the public.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.62.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1021/#laws.0.12.0">1969 c 1021 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/149/#laws.0.62.0">1976 c 149 s 62</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/582/#laws.0.10.0">1982 c 582 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.63">
        <h1 class="shn">17.63 REFUND OF FEES.</h1>
        <p>(a) Any producer, except a producer of potatoes in area number one, as listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.54#stat.17.54.9">17.54, subdivision 9</a>, a producer of wheat or barley, a producer of canola, or a producer of cultivated wild rice, may, by the use of forms to be provided by the commissioner and upon presentation of such proof as the commissioner requires, have the checkoff fee paid pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.51">17.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.69">17.69</a> fully or partially refunded, provided the checkoff fee was remitted on a timely basis. The request for refund must be received in the office of the commissioner within the time specified in the promotion order following the payment of the checkoff fee. In no event shall these requests for refund be accepted more often than 12 times per year. Refund shall be made by the commissioner and council within 30 days of the request for refund provided that the checkoff fee sought to be refunded has been received. Rules governing the refund of checkoff fees for all commodities shall be formulated by the commissioner, shall be fully outlined in the promotion order, and shall be available for the information of all producers concerned with the referendum.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner must allow partial refund requests from corn producers who have checked off and must allow for assignment of payment to the Minnesota Corn Growers Association if the Minnesota Corn Research and Promotion Council requests such action by the commissioner.</p>
        <p>(c) The Minnesota Corn Research and Promotion Council shall not elect to impose membership on any individual producer not requesting a partial refund or assignment of payment to the association.</p>
        <p>(d) For any wheat, barley, or cultivated wild rice for which the checkoff fee must be paid pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.51">17.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.69">17.69</a> and for which a checkoff fee or fee that serves a comparable purpose in a jurisdiction outside Minnesota had been previously paid for the same wheat, barley, or cultivated wild rice, the producer of the wheat, barley, or cultivated wild rice is exempt from payment of the checkoff fee. The commissioner, in consultation with the Wheat Research and Promotion Council, Barley Research and Promotion Council, and Cultivated Wild Rice Research and Promotion Council, shall determine jurisdictions outside of Minnesota which collect a checkoff fee or fee that serves a comparable purpose. In order to qualify for the exemption, the producer must demonstrate to the first purchaser that a checkoff fee or fee has been paid to such a jurisdiction.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.63.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1021/#laws.0.13.0">1969 c 1021 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/149/#laws.0.62.0">1976 c 149 s 62</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/582/#laws.0.11.0">1982 c 582 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/477/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 477 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/191/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 191 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/513/#laws.2.14.0">1992 c 513 art 2 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/452/#laws.0.4.0">1994 c 452 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.30.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/181/#laws.0.4.0">2014 c 181 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.64">
        <h1 class="shn">17.64 TERMINATION OF ORDER.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.64.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">By council.</span></h2>
          <p>The council after consultation with the commissioner and by a majority vote shall suspend or terminate a promotional order whenever it finds, after a public hearing, that an order is contrary to or does not effectuate the purposes or provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.51">17.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.69">17.69</a>, provided that the suspension or termination shall not become effective until the expiration of the current marketing year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.64.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">By referendum.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon petition of the same number of producers as required to initiate the promotional order, the commissioner shall within 60 days conduct a referendum to determine whether or not the promotional order shall be continued. The commissioner shall terminate the order at the end of the current marketing year if a majority of the producers voting in the referendum vote in favor of termination. The petition of producers shall include a statement certifying that the signatures are those of qualified producers of the commodity involved. The commissioner shall not conduct a referendum for termination of a promotional order if a referendum for termination of the same promotional order has been conducted within the preceding year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.64.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1021/#laws.0.14.0">1969 c 1021 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/149/#laws.0.62.0">1976 c 149 s 62</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/582/#laws.0.12.0">1982 c 582 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/187/#laws.3.7.0">1997 c 187 art 3 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.65"><b>17.65</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/582/#laws.0.14.0">1982 c 582 s 14</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.66">
        <h1 class="shn">17.66 PERMITTED ACTIVITIES.</h1>
        <p>No activity, including meetings, undertaken in pursuance of the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.51">17.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.69">17.69</a> and intended to benefit the producers, handlers and processors of such agricultural commodity shall be deemed or considered illegal or in restraint of trade.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.66.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1021/#laws.0.16.0">1969 c 1021 s 16</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.67">
        <h1 class="shn">17.67 PENALTY FOR VIOLATIONS.</h1>
        <p>Any person who violates any provision of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.51">17.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.69">17.69</a> or any rule of the commissioner promulgated pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.51">17.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.69">17.69</a> is guilty of a misdemeanor. Any first handler who fails to make collections or to file a return or to pay any assessment within the time required by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.51">17.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.69">17.69</a>, or who files a falsified return, shall be liable to the council for the amount due, plus a penalty of six percent of the amount due, plus one percent of the amount for each month of delay. If satisfied that the delay was excusable, the council may return all or any part of the penalty. Penalties shall be paid to the council and disposed of as provided with respect to other money collected under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.51">17.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.69">17.69</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.67.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1021/#laws.0.17.0">1969 c 1021 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/149/#laws.0.62.0">1976 c 149 s 62</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/582/#laws.0.13.0">1982 c 582 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.68"><b>17.68</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/582/#laws.0.14.0">1982 c 582 s 14</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.69">
        <h1 class="shn">17.69 NONLIABILITY OF STATE.</h1>
        <p>No liability shall be imposed upon the state of Minnesota for any acts or omissions of the commissioner or any council established pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.51">17.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.69">17.69</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.69.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1021/#laws.0.19.0">1969 c 1021 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/149/#laws.0.62.0">1976 c 149 s 62</a> subd 2</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">AGRICULTURAL MARKETING<br>AND BARGAINING</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.691">
        <h1 class="shn">17.691 CITATION.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.701">17.701</a> shall be known and may be cited as the "Agricultural Marketing and Bargaining Act of 1973."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.691.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/736/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 736 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.692">
        <h1 class="shn">17.692 DECLARATION OF POLICY.</h1>
        <p>Agricultural products are produced in Minnesota by many individual farmers, ranchers, and handlers scattered throughout the state. The efficient production and marketing of agricultural products by farmers, ranchers, and handlers is of vital concern to their welfare and to the general economy of Minnesota. The marketing and bargaining position of individual farmers, ranchers, and handlers will be adversely affected unless they are free to join together voluntarily in cooperative organizations as authorized by law. Interference with this right is contrary to the public interest, adversely affects the free and orderly flow of goods in interstate and foreign commerce, and affects the welfare of the people of Minnesota. It is, therefore, declared to be the policy of this state and the purpose of this chapter to establish standards of fair practices required of handlers and producers in their dealings in agricultural products and to encourage settlement of disputes between handlers and producers of agricultural products.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.692.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/736/#laws.0.2.0">1973 c 736 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/373/#laws.0.2.0">1998 c 373 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.693">
        <h1 class="shn">17.693 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.693.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a>, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.693.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Association; association of producers.</span></h2>
          <p>"Association" or "association of producers" means an association of producers of agricultural commodities engaged in marketing, bargaining, shipping, or processing an agricultural commodity into agricultural products on behalf of its members who are producers of the agricultural commodity, which has been accredited by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.693.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.693.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Producer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Producer" means any person, who in any one calendar year within the previous two calendar years, produces or causes to be produced any agricultural commodity in quantity beyond the person's own family use, and who is able to transfer, during the calendar year, to a handler or an association a merchantable title to the agricultural commodity or provide management, labor, machinery, facilities, or any other production input, with the assumption of risk, for the production of the agricultural commodity under a written contract.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.693.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Agricultural commodity.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agricultural commodity" includes all agricultural goods produced under contract for marketing as defined by the commissioner of agriculture. It does not include any commodity sold by a producer to another producer for the other's own exclusive use and not for resale. The kinds, types and subtypes of products to be classed together as an agricultural commodity for the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.701">17.701</a> shall be determined by the commissioner on the basis of common usage and practice.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.693.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Handler.</span></h2>
          <p>"Handler" means a person, other than an association, engaged in the business or practice of acquiring agricultural commodities from producers or associations for processing or sale; grading, packaging, handling, storing, or processing agricultural commodities received from producers or associations; contracting or negotiating contracts or other arrangements with or on behalf of producers or associations with respect to the production of any agricultural commodity; or acting as an agent or broker for a handler in the performance of any function or act specified above. It does not include a producer who sells at a retail establishment which the producer owns and operates or who sells at a produce market, agricultural commodities produced by the producer and agricultural commodities produced by another producer subject to value limitation established by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.693.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.693.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Marketing year.</span></h2>
          <p>"Marketing year" shall mean, generally, any time between the second day of February of the previous calendar year and the first day of February of the subsequent year, unless the commissioner shall determine an alternative time period for a specific agricultural commodity to be designated as its marketing year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.693.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/736/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 736 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/373/#laws.0.3.0">1998 c 373 s 3</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.57.0">2001 c 161 s 57</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.694">
        <h1 class="shn">17.694 ACCREDITATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.694.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>Any association accredited under this section may engage in bargaining as provided for under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a>.</p>
          <p>(1) An association desiring accreditation shall file with the commissioner in the form required by the commissioner. The request shall contain properly certified evidence that the association meets the standards for accreditation and shall be accompanied by a report of the names and addresses of member producers, the name of each handler to whom the member producer delivered or contracted to deliver the agricultural commodity during the previous two calendar years. A fee to cover the costs of the commissioner in processing the request shall be established pursuant to chapter 14, and paid by the association when the request is filed.</p>
          <p>(2) The commissioner shall notify all handlers named in the request for accreditation of an association of producers. The notice must be sent to the handlers named in the request by first class mail within ten days of the commissioner receiving the request for accreditation. The commissioner shall maintain records indicating the date of mailing.</p>
          <p>(3) The commissioner may require all handlers of an agricultural commodity produced in a bargaining unit area as individuals to file within 30 days following a request, a report, properly certified, showing the correct names and addresses of all producers of the agricultural commodity who have delivered the agricultural commodity to the handler during the two calendar years preceding the filing of the report.</p>
          <p>(4) Data submitted to the commissioner by producer associations under clause (1) and by commodity handlers under clause (3) are private data on individuals or nonpublic data, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.9">13.02, subdivision 9</a> or 12.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.694.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Bargaining unit determination.</span></h2>
          <p>In determination of accreditation, the commissioner shall determine the appropriate bargaining unit by plant, but may define the bargaining unit by processor or company if there is a history of identical contracts offered to producers on a processor or companywide basis. This determination shall be the unit area for the bargaining provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a> as is applicable to associations and handlers. In making a determination, the commissioner shall define as appropriate the bargaining unit area in terms of the agricultural commodity produced, the definition of the agricultural commodity, geographic area covered, and number of producers included as is consistent with the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) the community of interest of the producers included;</p>
          <p>(2) the potential serious conflicts of interests among members of the proposed unit;</p>
          <p>(3) the effect of exclusions on the capacity of the association to effectively bargain for the bargaining unit as defined;</p>
          <p>(4) the kinds, types, and subtypes of products to be classed together as agricultural commodity for which the bargaining unit is proposed;</p>
          <p>(5) whether the producers eligible for membership in the proposed bargaining unit meet the definition of "producer" for the agricultural commodity involved;</p>
          <p>(6) the wishes of the producers; and</p>
          <p>(7) the pattern of past marketing of the commodity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.694.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Standards.</span></h2>
          <p>An association shall be accredited only if it complies with the following:</p>
          <p>(a) The association meets the requirements of the Capper-Volstead Act, United States Code, title 7, section 291-2.</p>
          <p>(b) The association has submitted a copy of its bylaws which provide that: Each member of the association shall have one vote in all votes of the membership of the association; that officers or directors shall be elected by a majority of the members voting or by delegates representing a majority of the membership; and that all elections shall be by secret ballot.</p>
          <p>(c) The association would have marketing and bargaining contracts for the current or next marketing year with more than 50 percent of the producers of an agricultural commodity who are in the bargaining unit area. An association whose main purpose is bargaining but which processes a surplus into a form which is not the subject of bargaining is not a processing cooperative. The contracts with members shall specify the agricultural commodity and that the members have appointed the association as their exclusive agent in negotiations with handlers for prices and other terms of trade with respect to the sale and marketing of the agricultural commodity and obligate the members of the association to dispose of their production or holdings of the agricultural commodity through or at the direction of the association.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.694.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Accreditation determination.</span></h2>
          <p>Within 60 days of the filing date of the request for accreditation by an association, the commissioner shall determine whether the association shall be accredited. If the commissioner determines that insufficient evidence was filed by the association, the commissioner may permit the association to file an amended request for accreditation within 30 days following the determination and notification of the association. The commissioner shall then determine, within 30 days of the filing of the amended request, whether the association shall be accredited. An association which is denied accreditation after filing of an amended request may not file another request for accreditation for a period of one year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.694.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Hearing; appeal.</span></h2>
          <p>Within 30 days of a decision by the commissioner denying accreditation to an association, the association may request a hearing before the commissioner. The commissioner shall then conduct a hearing to determine whether the association shall be accredited. This hearing shall be governed by the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.57">14.57</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.60">14.60</a>. If the commissioner, after this hearing has been held, determines that the association should not be accredited, the association may appeal this decision in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.63">14.63</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.69">14.69</a>. Only an association denied certification after such hearing may appeal under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.694.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Date of accreditation.</span></h2>
          <p>Accreditation of the association by the commissioner shall be effective upon receipt by the association of the notice of accreditation from the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.694.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Revocation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall consider revocation of accreditation upon any of the following conditions:</p>
          <p>(a) Upon receipt of a request from an accredited association for its own disaccreditation.</p>
          <p>(b) Upon receipt of a petition requesting that the accredited association be disaccredited and bearing the signatures of at least ten percent of the producers of an accredited association in the bargaining unit. Within ten days following the receipt of a petition bearing the signatures of at least ten percent of the producers of an accredited association in a bargaining unit, the commissioner shall order a referendum, to take place at least seven days but not more than 20 days after the order, among the members of the accredited association and if in the referendum 50 percent of association members approve, the association accreditation shall be revoked by the commissioner. The commissioner shall have representatives of the department present at the referendum vote to conduct the referendum and take action to prevent unfair practices by the association of producers or handlers to obstruct or influence voting. Tabulation of the vote is the responsibility of the department. The department may adopt rules governing any referendum for repeal of accreditation. A request for a revocation of accreditation may occur only during the first three months of a marketing year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.694.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Representation.</span></h2>
          <p>The accredited association shall represent all member producers who are in the bargaining unit area and it shall act as exclusive sales agents for the members of the accredited association in the bargaining unit area in negotiations with handlers. The association may not assess, bargain for, or claim to represent those producers who choose not to be represented by the association or choose not to have a bargaining committee bargain for them.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.694.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/736/#laws.0.4.0">1973 c 736 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/88/#laws.0.1.0">1975 c 88 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/373/#laws.0.6.0">1998 c 373 s 6</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.57.0">2001 c 161 s 57</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.695">
        <h1 class="shn">17.695 MARKETING AND BARGAINING COMMITTEE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.695.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>After accreditation of the association, the association shall establish and authorize a marketing and bargaining committee to negotiate, as the association's exclusive agent, with handlers for the sale and marketing of the agricultural commodity for which the association was accredited.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.695.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>This committee shall be comprised of members of the association elected by the association in a secret ballot election, except that the association may contract with legal counsel who shall, at the discretion of the association, be eligible for membership on the committee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.695.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>The production of the agricultural commodity shall comprise a significant portion of the total producing operation of each committee member.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.695.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Ineligible members.</span></h2>
          <p>Members who have any quantity contracted with a producer owned and controlled processing cooperative are not eligible to serve on a marketing and bargaining committee for such a commodity.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.695.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/736/#laws.0.5.0">1973 c 736 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.696">
        <h1 class="shn">17.696 UNFAIR PRACTICES OF HANDLERS AND ASSOCIATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.696.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Unfair practices by handler.</span></h2>
          <p>Producers of agricultural commodities are free to join together voluntarily in associations as authorized by law without interference by handlers. A handler shall not engage in any of the following practices, defined as unfair practices:</p>
          <p>(a) To coerce a producer in the exercise of the right to join and belong to or to refrain from joining or belonging to an association or to refuse to deal with a producer because of the exercise of the right to join and belong to an association.</p>
          <p>(b) To discriminate against a producer with respect to price, quantity, quality or other terms of purchase, acquisition or other handling of agricultural products because of membership in or contract with an association.</p>
          <p>(c) To coerce or intimidate a producer to breach, cancel or terminate a membership agreement or marketing contract with an association or a contract with a handler.</p>
          <p>(d) To pay or loan money, give anything of value or offer any other inducement or reward to a producer for refusing or ceasing to belong to an association.</p>
          <p>(e) To make or circulate unsubstantiated reports about the finances, management or activities of associations or other handlers.</p>
          <p>(f) To conspire, combine, agree or arrange with any other person to do or aid or abet the doing of any practice which is in violation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.701">17.701</a>.</p>
          <p>(g) To refuse to bargain with an association with whom the handler has had prior dealings or with an association whose producers in the bargaining units have had dealings with the handler prior to July 1, 1973.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.696.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Unfair practices by association.</span></h2>
          <p>An association shall not engage nor permit an employee or agent to engage in the following practices, defined as unfair practices:</p>
          <p>(1) coerce a producer in the exercise of the right to join and belong to or to refrain from joining or belonging to an association or refuse to deal with a producer because of the exercise of the right to join and belong to or refrain from joining an association;</p>
          <p>(2) enter into a contract which discriminates against a producer represented by that association;</p>
          <p>(3) act in a manner contrary to the bylaws of the association;</p>
          <p>(4) coerce or intimidate a handler to breach, cancel, or terminate an agreement or marketing contract with an association or a contract with a producer;</p>
          <p>(5) make or circulate unsubstantiated reports about the finances, management, or activities of other associations or handlers; or</p>
          <p>(6) conspire, combine, agree, or arrange with another person to do or aid or abet the doing of any practice which is in violation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.696.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/736/#laws.0.6.0">1973 c 736 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/373/#laws.0.11.0">1998 c 373 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.57.0">2001 c 161 s 57</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.697">
        <h1 class="shn">17.697 INFORMATIONAL EXCHANGES; DISPUTE RESOLUTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a>, "informational exchange" means the mutual obligation of a handler and an association or their designated representatives to meet at a mutually agreed-upon time in conformance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a> and confer and provide information about their expectations for the upcoming marketing year. The informational exchange must be a serious, fair, and reasonable attempt to reach agreement by acknowledging or refuting with reason points brought up by either party with respect to the terms and conditions of a contract relative to trading between handlers and producers of the agricultural commodity. The topics may include, but are not limited to, the following:</p>
          <p>(1) prices and terms of sale;</p>
          <p>(2) quality specifications;</p>
          <p>(3) quantity to be marketed by acreage or weight;</p>
          <p>(4) transactions involving products and services utilized by one party and provided by the other party; and</p>
          <p>(5) checkoff procedures pursuant to assessments levied by the association whereby a portion of the producers' annual production payments under a contract are collected by handlers from producers within the bargaining unit and paid to the association on some other arrangement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">First two meetings.</span></h2>
          <p>The handler and an association of producers or their designated representatives shall meet at least two times for informational exchanges prior to 60 days before the beginning of the marketing year. Neither party, however, is required to disclose proprietary business or financial records or information. Both parties shall inform the department in writing of the time of both informational exchanges at least ten days prior to the first meeting. Verification of completion of training in negotiation, as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a>, must be included with the notification sent to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Continuing negotiations.</span></h2>
          <p>After the conclusion of the second informational exchange and no agreement is reached, negotiations may continue between the parties at mutually agreed-upon times. Mediation may be requested in accordance with this section by any party.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Agreement not required.</span></h2>
          <p>The parties may reach agreement for a contract during the informational exchanges. However, the obligation to meet for informational exchanges does not require either party to agree to a proposal, to make a concession, or to enter into a contract.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">If no agreement is reached.</span></h2>
          <p>If an agreement is not reached during the informational exchanges, negotiations must be considered to continue and either party may request mediation as provided in this section. Negotiations may continue without mediation and an agreement may be reached without the use of mediation. Negotiations must be a serious, fair, and reasonable attempt to reach agreement by acknowledging or refuting with reason points brought up by either party with respect to the terms and conditions of a contract relative to trading between handlers and producers of the agricultural commodity. A request for mediation requires both parties to the negotiation to complete the mediation process described in this section, but does not obligate either party to agree to a proposal, to make a concession, or to enter into a contract. However, the parties are required to perform according to any agreement reached at the conclusion of the mediation process.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Mediation request.</span></h2>
          <p>An association of producers or a handler may request mediation only within ten days after the second informational exchange meeting. Written notice requesting mediation must be mailed to the commissioner and postmarked within ten days of the second informational exchange, with a copy to the nonrequesting party, and the notice for mediation must contain the last offer made by the party requesting mediation. Within three days after receiving the request for mediation, the commissioner shall require the nonrequesting party to provide reasons for rejecting the last offer made by the requesting party and revisions to the last offer that might be required to reach an agreement. The nonrequesting party will have five days from the date of the postmark to provide a response to the commissioner and also provide a copy of the response to the requesting party. The commissioner shall request the American Arbitration Association or a comparable dispute resolution organization to make available a list of at least three qualified mediators, but not more than six, for the parties to select one individual to mediate the dispute. Qualified mediators are those who have met the training requirements of Rule <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/court_rules/gp/id/114/">114.12</a> of the Minnesota General Rules of Practice for the District Courts, are familiar with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a>, and have served as mediator in at least three other commercial disputes or have commensurate experience. The handler and the association may agree on a mediator or, failing agreement, the commissioner may select the mediator from the list provided by allowing each party to strike one mediator and choosing one from the remaining names on the list.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Mediation rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The American Arbitration Association mediation rules must be followed during the mediation process. If there is a conflict between those rules and this statute, the statute prevails. Any information shared in the mediation process or offers to settle are to be considered confidential and must not be used against either party in any other proceeding, court action, or dispute resolution process unless otherwise discoverable from outside of the mediation process.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Duration of mediation.</span></h2>
          <p>The mediation process must conclude not more than 20 days after the mediator has been selected and notified by the department. If the mediator feels that additional time may result in an agreement between the parties, the mediator may extend the mediation process for an additional five days. However, the mediation must conclude, under any circumstance, no later than 15 days prior to the start of the marketing year, unless the parties agree to a different date, but no later than the first day of the marketing year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Mediation costs.</span></h2>
          <p>All costs for retaining a mediator and proceedings during the mediation process must be shared equally by both parties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Subpoenas.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner has the subpoena authority to compel participation in the mediation process for either party after the informational exchanges.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Enforceability.</span></h2>
          <p>Any final written agreement reached during the mediation procedure is enforceable under the law and in the courts of this state. The parties are not required to reach an agreement, but they are required to proceed through the mediation process as outlined in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Binding arbitration.</span></h2>
          <p>If an agreement is not reached during the mediation process, and upon written consent by both parties, binding arbitration as set forth in this chapter may be used to create a contract or resolve the dispute.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Notification.</span></h2>
          <p>The parties shall each notify the commissioner after the end of the mediation period, if an agreement has not been reached, of their desire to use binding arbitration to settle the dispute. An arbitrator must be selected as provided in subdivision 18. The notification must include its final offer in which it shall identify all matters as to which the parties agree with contractual language setting forth these agreements and all matters as to which the parties do not agree with contractual language setting forth the party's final offer for resolution of those disagreements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Process.</span></h2>
          <p>For all matters submitted to arbitration, the arbitrator may choose between the final offers of the parties or fashion a different solution between, but not exceeding, the final offers of the parties. If the parties reach an agreement on the matters under arbitration before the arbitrator issues a decision, they may submit a joint final offer that the arbitrator shall accept and render as the decision. The arbitrator may hold hearings and administer oaths, examine witnesses and documents, take testimony and receive evidence, and issue subpoenas to compel the attendance of witnesses and the production of records. A person who fails to obey the subpoena of an arbitrator may be punished for contempt of court on application by the arbitrator to the district court for the county in which the failure occurs. The arbitrator may use other information in addition to that provided by or elicited from the parties. The arbitrator shall issue a decision within ten days of the commencement of arbitration and that decision is binding on the parties. If the parties reach an agreement on the matters in the arbitrator's decision prior to signing the contract, they may submit a joint final offer to the arbitrator. The arbitrator shall rescind the previous decision and accept and render the joint final offer as the decision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Contract.</span></h2>
          <p>Within five days after the arbitrator's decision, the handler shall prepare a contract that must include all terms agreed to by the parties in bargaining or awarded in arbitration and shall present the contract to the association of producers who must accept the terms of the contract within five days of its presentation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">List of arbitrators.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner, in consultation with the American Arbitration Association or comparable dispute resolution organization, shall establish a list of arbitrators who are qualified by education, training, and experience to carry out the responsibilities of an arbitrator under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Costs of arbitration.</span></h2>
          <p>All costs of arbitration must be borne equally by the parties. The arbitrator shall submit a statement of charges and expenses to the parties and to the commissioner. Each party shall pay the arbitrator directly.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.697.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Selection of arbitrator.</span></h2>
          <p>The arbitrator must be selected by the commissioner. The commissioner shall submit a list composed of the names of three persons knowledgeable in the marketing of the agricultural commodity from which the arbitrator must be chosen. Qualified arbitrators are those who have met the training requirements of <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/court_rules/gp/id/114/#114.12">Rule 114.12</a> of the Minnesota General Rules of Practice for the District Courts, are familiar with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a>, and have served as an arbitrator in at least three other commercial disputes or have commensurate experience. The selection must be made by the association representative and the handler representative, each striking one name from the list. If two names remain, the commissioner shall decide which one is the arbitrator.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.697.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/736/#laws.0.7.0">1973 c 736 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/88/#laws.0.3.0">1975 c 88 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/373/#laws.0.12.0">1998 c 373 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.57.0">2001 c 161 s 57</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.698">
        <h1 class="shn">17.698 FACTORS TO BE CONSIDERED IN MEDIATION AND ARBITRATION.</h1>
        <p>All decisions of mediation and arbitration which result from section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.697">17.697</a> must consider the following factors:</p>
        <p>(1) prices or projected prices for the agricultural commodity paid by the competing handlers in the market area or competing market areas worldwide;</p>
        <p>(2) amount of the commodity produced or projections of production in the production area or competing marketing areas worldwide;</p>
        <p>(3) relationship between the quantity produced and the quantity handled by the handler;</p>
        <p>(4) the producers cost of production including the cost which would be involved in paying farm labor a fair wage rate and providing them with adequate housing;</p>
        <p>(5) the efficiency of farm operations of similar size and the projected prices of alternative agricultural commodities grown in the market area;</p>
        <p>(6) the cost of production of similar sized handlers;</p>
        <p>(7) the average consumer prices for goods and services, commonly known as the cost of living;</p>
        <p>(8) the component of the agricultural commodity that makes up the producer's income;</p>
        <p>(9) the impact of the award on the competitive position of the handler in the marketing area or competing areas worldwide;</p>
        <p>(10) the impact of the award on the competitive position of the agricultural commodity in relationship to competing commodities;</p>
        <p>(11) a fair return on investment;</p>
        <p>(12) kind, quality, or grade of the commodity involved;</p>
        <p>(13) stipulation of the parties; and</p>
        <p>(14) other factors which are normally or traditionally taken into consideration in determining prices, quality, quantity, and the costs of other services involved.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.698.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/736/#laws.0.8.0">1973 c 736 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/373/#laws.0.13.0">1998 c 373 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.699"><b>17.699</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/373/#laws.0.20.0">1998 c 373 s 20</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.70">
        <h1 class="shn">17.70 VIOLATION PROCEDURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.70.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's duties.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purpose of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a>, the commissioner may receive complaints with respect to violations or threatened violations. The commissioner may make all necessary investigations, examinations or inspections of any violation or threatened violation specified in the sworn complaint filed with the commissioner. If, upon such investigation, the commissioner considers that there is reasonable cause to believe that the person charged has committed a practice in violation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a>, the commissioner shall issue and cause to be served a complaint upon the person. The complaint shall summon the person to a hearing before the commissioner at the time and place fixed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.70.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Findings of fact; order.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner determines that the person complained of has committed a practice in violation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a>, the commissioner shall state findings of fact and shall issue and cause to be served on the person an order to cease the violation and shall order further affirmative action as will effectuate the policies of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.70.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Dismissal.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner is of the opinion that the person complained of has not committed a practice in violation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a>, the commissioner shall make findings of fact and issue an order dismissing the complaint.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.70.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Modification or set aside.</span></h2>
          <p>Until the record in a case has been filed in a court the commissioner may, at any time upon reasonable notice and in such manner as the commissioner deems proper, modify or set aside, in whole or in part, any finding or order made or issued, with jurisdiction for such a change specified in additional findings of fact.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.70.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may request the attorney general of the state of Minnesota to seek the appropriate temporary relief or restraining order of injunction in district court to insure the enforcement of the commissioner's findings.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.70.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/736/#laws.0.10.0">1973 c 736 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/373/#laws.0.14.0">1998 c 373 s 14</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.57.0">2001 c 161 s 57</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.701">
        <h1 class="shn">17.701 RULES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may promulgate rules necessary for the administration of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a> in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a> and chapter 14.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.701.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/736/#laws.0.11.0">1973 c 736 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/373/#laws.0.17.0">1998 c 373 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.57.0">2001 c 161 s 57</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.702">
        <h1 class="shn">17.702 NEGOTIATION CLASSES REQUIRED.</h1>
        <p>Upon accreditation of an association of producers, at least two members of bargaining teams from both the association and the handler named in the application must attend instructional classes covering negotiation, mediation, arbitration, and facilitation approved by the commissioner. The instruction period must be at least three hours. The informational exchanges may not commence unless certificates of completion are on file with the commissioner. After an association has been accredited for a period exceeding one year, the association and the handler must have at least one member of their bargaining team in informational exchanges or negotiations complete training in the previous year of at least three hours in negotiation, mediation, arbitration, and facilitation, as described in this section, before the dates for informational exchanges are determined. Verification of training must accompany the notification to the commissioner that informational exchanges are scheduled.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.702.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/373/#laws.0.18.0">1998 c 373 s 18</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.703"><b>17.703</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.58.0">2001 c 161 s 58</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">DISCLOSURE OF PRODUCTION CONTRACT TERMS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.710">
        <h1 class="shn">17.710 AGRICULTURAL CONTRACTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) A production or marketing contract entered into, renewed, or amended on or after July 1, 2024, between an agricultural producer and a processor, marketer, or other purchaser of agricultural products, including a cooperative organized under chapter 308A or 308B, must not contain provisions that prohibit the producer from disclosing terms, conditions, and prices contained in the contract. Any provision prohibiting disclosure by the producer is void.</p>
        <p>(b) A contract entered into, renewed, or amended on or after July 1, 2023, between an agricultural producer and an entity buying, selling, certifying, or otherwise participating in a market for stored carbon must not contain provisions that prohibit the producer from disclosing terms, conditions, and prices contained in the contract. Any provision prohibiting disclosure by the producer is void.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.710.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.32.0">1999 c 231 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/43/#laws.2.12.0">2023 c 43 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/126/#laws.2.12.0">2024 c 126 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.38.12.0">2024 c 127 art 38 s 12</a></p>
      </div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.728"><b>17.728</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/326/#laws.6.34.0">1989 c 326 art 6 s 34</a>]</div>
