      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.76"><b>17.76</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.58.0">2001 c 161 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.162.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 162</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">AGRICULTURAL LAND PRESERVATION AND CONSERVATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.80">
        <h1 class="shn">17.80 STATE AGRICULTURAL LAND PRESERVATION AND CONSERVATION POLICY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.80.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Policy.</span></h2>
          <p>It is the policy of the state to preserve agricultural land and conserve its long-term use for the production of food and other agricultural products by:</p>
          <p>(a) Protection of agricultural land and certain parcels of open space land from conversion to other uses;</p>
          <p>(b) Conservation and enhancement of soil and water resources to ensure their long-term quality and productivity;</p>
          <p>(c) Encouragement of planned growth and development of urban and rural areas to ensure the most effective use of agricultural land, resources and capital; and</p>
          <p>(d) Fostering of ownership and operation of agricultural land by resident farmers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.80.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Methods.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislature finds that the policy in subdivision 1 will be best met by:</p>
          <p>(a) Defining and locating lands well suited for the production of agricultural and forest products, and the use of that information as part of any local planning and zoning decision;</p>
          <p>(b) Providing local units of government with coordinating guidelines, tools and incentives to prevent the unplanned and unscheduled conversion of agricultural and open space land to other uses;</p>
          <p>(c) Providing relief from escalating property taxes and special assessments and protection of normal farm operations in agricultural areas subject to development pressures;</p>
          <p>(d) Development of state policy to increase implementation of soil and water conservation by farmers;</p>
          <p>(e) Assuring that state agencies act to maximize the preservation and conservation of agricultural land and minimize the disruption of agricultural production, in accordance with local social, economic and environmental considerations of the agricultural community;</p>
          <p>(f) Assuring that public agencies employ and promote the use of management procedures which maintain or enhance the productivity of lands well suited to the production of food and other agricultural products;</p>
          <p>(g) Guiding the orderly development and maintenance of transportation systems in rural Minnesota while preserving agricultural land to the greatest possible extent;</p>
          <p>(h) Guiding the orderly construction and development of energy generation and transmission systems and enhancing the development of alternative energy to meet the needs of rural and urban communities and preserve agricultural land to the greatest possible extent by reducing energy costs and minimizing the use of agricultural land for energy production facilities; and</p>
          <p>(i) Guiding the orderly development of solid and hazardous waste management sites to meet the needs and safety of rural and urban communities and preserve agricultural land to the greatest possible extent by minimizing the use of agricultural land for waste management sites.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.80.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/512/#laws.0.1.0">1982 c 512 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.81">
        <h1 class="shn">17.81 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.81.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.80">17.80</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.84">17.84</a>, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.81.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Action which adversely affects.</span></h2>
          <p>"Action which adversely affects" means any of the following actions taken in respect to agricultural land which have or would have the effect of substantially restricting the agricultural use of the land: (1) acquisition for a nonagricultural use except acquisition for any unit of the outdoor recreation system described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.05">86A.05</a>, other than a trail described in subdivision 4 of that section; (2) granting of a permit, license, franchise or other official authorization for nonagricultural use; (3) lease of state-owned land for nonagricultural use except for mineral exploration or mining; or (4) granting or loaning of state funds for purposes which are not consistent with agricultural use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.81.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Agricultural land.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agricultural land" means land which is in agricultural use, and which has been identified as agricultural land by a local unit of government pursuant to Minnesota Statutes 2020, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/366.10">366.10</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/366.181">366.181</a>, or sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/394.21">394.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/394.37">394.37</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/462.351">462.351</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/462.364">462.364</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473H.04">473H.04</a>, or which is composed of predominantly class I, II, III, or IV soils as identified in the land capability classification system of the United States Department of Agriculture Natural Resources Conservation Service and the county soil survey, if completed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.81.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Agricultural use.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agricultural use" means use of land for the production of livestock, dairy animals, dairy products, poultry and poultry products, fur bearing animals, horticultural and nursery stock which is under chapter 18H, fruit of all kinds, vegetables, forage, grains, bees, and apiary products. Wetlands, pasture and woodlands accompanying land in agricultural use shall be considered to be in agricultural use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.81.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agency" means a department identified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.01">15.01</a>, and any other agency of the state whose actions are by virtue of other law governed by the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.80">17.80</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.84">17.84</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.81.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.81.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/512/#laws.0.2.0">1982 c 512 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/13/#laws.3.7.0">2007 c 13 art 3 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/21/#laws.1.109.0">2015 c 21 art 1 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.4.0">2023 c 25 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.82">
        <h1 class="shn">17.82 PROHIBITED AGENCY ACTION.</h1>
        <p>Any agency action which the agency determines will adversely affect ten acres or more of agricultural land shall be referred to the commissioner to be reviewed and acted upon as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.84">17.84</a>. No agency shall take any action which adversely affects ten acres or more of agricultural land without first attempting to find alternative methods or locations for the action or otherwise attempting to reduce the adverse affects. If, after evaluating the alternatives, the agency determines that the benefit to the state from preserving the agricultural use of the land is less than the cost of implementing an alternative action, the agency shall inform the commissioner of that determination in writing.</p>
        <p>An agency action is not subject to review under this section or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.84">17.84</a> if the action is reviewed as required by chapter 116D and the environmental review rules adopted under that chapter, or if a political subdivision is required by law to review and approve the action.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.82.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/512/#laws.0.3.0">1982 c 512 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.83"><b>17.83</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.57.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 57</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.84">
        <h1 class="shn">17.84 DUTIES OF COMMISSIONER.</h1>
        <p>Within 30 days of the receipt of the notice provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.82">17.82</a>, the commissioner shall review the agency's proposed action, shall negotiate with the agency, and shall recommend to the agency in writing the implementation either of the action as proposed or an alternative. In making recommendations, the commissioner shall follow the statement of policy contained in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.80">17.80</a>. If the agency receives no response from the commissioner within 30 days, it shall be deemed a recommendation that the agency take the action as proposed.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.84.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/512/#laws.0.5.0">1982 c 512 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.35.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 35</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LIVESTOCK PRODUCTION POLICY</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.844">
        <h1 class="shn">17.844 LIVESTOCK PRODUCTION POLICY.</h1>
        <p>(a) The policy of the state is to promote livestock production on family farms under a broad range of management systems that are environmentally sound and meet all legal requirements of all jurisdictions, including federal, state, county, town, city, and watershed district requirements.</p>
        <p>(b) In order to promote livestock production on family farms, state agencies when appropriate shall, to the extent allowed by law:</p>
        <p>(1) promote the establishment of livestock enterprises on family farms;</p>
        <p>(2) promote environmental protection and water quality improvement through increased livestock production that results in controlling runoff through increased acreage of hay, pasture, and small grains; and</p>
        <p>(3) promote more farms to use agronomically applied manure to increase the water holding capacity of the soil and control erosion.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.844.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/254/#laws.0.2.0">2004 c 254 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">AGRICULTURAL LABORATORY SERVICES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.85">
        <h1 class="shn">17.85 LABORATORY SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.85.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Account.</span></h2>
          <p>A laboratory services account is established in the agricultural fund. Payments for laboratory services performed by the Laboratory Services Division of the Department of Agriculture must be deposited in the agricultural fund and credited to the laboratory services account. Money in the account, including interest earned on the account, is annually appropriated to the commissioner of agriculture to administer the programs of the Laboratory Services Division.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.85.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agriculture laboratory.</span></h2>
          <p>The agriculture laboratory exists to provide analytical and technical services in support of agency programs that protect and enhance the states' agriculture, environment, and food chain. The laboratory may provide analytical and technical services for a fee to any public or private entity as requested or required to meet department objectives in support of Minnesota agriculture and a national food safety system.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.85.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/688/#laws.20.1.0">1988 c 688 art 20 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.33.0">1999 c 231 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.31.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">URBAN FOREST PROMOTION<br>AND DEVELOPMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.86">
        <h1 class="shn">17.86 URBAN FOREST PROMOTION AND DEVELOPMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.86.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Overall.</span></h2>
          <p>The following course of action is intended as a basis for prompt action to the maximum extent practical within the limits of state, local, and commercial resources. The assignment of a basic responsibility to a particular agency is not intended to confer exclusive responsibility or authority unless specifically stated, for joint action is intended as the key to a successful program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.86.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Research.</span></h2>
          <p>The University of Minnesota and its Extension Service in cooperation with the commissioner of agriculture shall institute a continuing research program on tree varieties most suitable for growth within the state; and the proper placement of individual trees and groups of trees in new or existing commercial, industrial, and residential settings to maximize energy saving benefits. The University of Minnesota and the commissioner shall work closely with nurseries and other suppliers of trees to assure a constant and reliable supply of the desirable varieties is available for planting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.86.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Information.</span></h2>
          <p>The University of Minnesota Extension Service, in cooperation with the commissioners of agriculture, education, natural resources, and commerce, shall serve as the principal agency for publishing and circulating information derived from research under subdivision 2 among the various municipalities and individual property owners in the state. Where practical, the Extension Service and the State Energy Office in the Department of Commerce shall secure the advice and assistance of various energy utilities interested and concerned with conservation. The commissioner of agriculture shall establish an information source for requests for nursery stock, to match needs of municipalities with stocks of trees available for planting from private and governmental sources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.86.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Transportation plantings.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of transportation shall utilize information on varieties and placement of trees to provide maximum forestation in rest areas and other areas controlled by the department. The commissioner of transportation should consider the use of trees in conjunction with solid noise walls along urban freeways to the maximum extent practical.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.86.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">School Arbor Day activities.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioners of education, agriculture, and natural resources, with the state Arbor Month Committee and its individual public and private members, shall jointly work to expand and strengthen programs available to all levels of schools in forestry education and shall encourage reinstitution of Arbor Day activities. Information on desirable shade tree varieties and efficient spacing and location of shade trees shall be made available for use in related adult education courses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.86.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Municipal action.</span></h2>
          <p>A city of the first or second class shall, by ordinance, require of the developer the use of properly placed trees in new subdivisions and plantings on lands dedicated to parks and open spaces. Cities of the third and fourth class may adopt such ordinances. Counties may assist and encourage the smaller cities in tree planting programs. A municipality may contract on a long-term basis with nurseries and shade tree wholesalers to assure continued availability of nursery stock of the desirable shade tree varieties.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.86.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/445/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 445 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/4/#laws.6.11.0">1Sp2001 c 4 art 6 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">MERCURY MANOMETERS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.861">
        <h1 class="shn">17.861 REPLACEMENT OF MERCURY MANOMETERS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner, in cooperation with the Pollution Control Agency, dairy equipment manufacturers and suppliers, and other interested parties, shall develop a program to provide replacement nonmercury manometers for a $50 fee and to arrange for the acceptance, disposal, and recycling of the mercury, apparatus, and manometers at no cost to the dairy farmer. The mercury, manometers, and apparatus shall be managed in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.932">115A.932</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.92">116.92</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.861.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.26.0">1997 c 216 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">AGRICULTURAL CONTRACTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.90">
        <h1 class="shn">17.90 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.90.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.90">17.90</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.98">17.98</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.90.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Agricultural contract.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agricultural contract" means any written contract between a contractor and a producer, but does not include a contract between a grain buyer licensed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/223.17#stat.223.17.1">223.17, subdivision 1</a>, who purchases grain from a producer as a merchant or seller of grain and does not contract with the producer to grow or raise the crops producing the grain.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.90.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agricultural commodity.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agricultural commodity" means a material produced for use in or as food, feed, seed, or fiber and includes crops for fiber, food, oilseeds, seeds, livestock, livestock products, poultry, poultry products, and other products or by-products of the farm produced for the same or similar use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.90.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Contractor.</span></h2>
          <p>"Contractor" means a person who in the ordinary course of business buys agricultural commodities grown or raised in this state or who contracts with a producer to grow or raise agricultural commodities in this state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.90.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 3c]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.90.3b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3b.<span class="headnote">Grain.</span></h2>
          <p>"Grain" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/223.16#stat.223.16.4">223.16, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.90.3c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3c.<span class="headnote">Legible type.</span></h2>
          <p>"Legible type" means a typeface at least as large as ten-point modern type, one-point leaded.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.90.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Producer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Producer" means a person who produces or causes to be produced an agricultural commodity in a quantity beyond the person's own family use and:</p>
          <p>(1) is able to transfer title to another; or</p>
          <p>(2) provides management, labor, machinery, facilities, or any other production input for the production of an agricultural commodity.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.90.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/517/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 517 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/470/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 470 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/373/#laws.0.4.0">2002 c 373 s 4</a>,5</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.91">
        <h1 class="shn">17.91 REQUIRED LANGUAGE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.91.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Mediation; arbitration.</span></h2>
          <p>A contract for an agricultural commodity between a contractor and a producer must contain language providing for resolution of contract disputes by either mediation or arbitration. If there is a contract dispute, either party may make a written request to the commissioner for mediation or arbitration services as specified in the contract, to facilitate resolution of the dispute.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.91.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Written disclosure of risks.</span></h2>
          <p>An agricultural contract must be accompanied by a clear written disclosure setting forth the nature of the material risks faced by the producer if the producer enters into the contract. The statement must meet the plain language requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>. The statement may be in the form of a written statement or checklist and may be developed in cooperation with producers or producer organizations. A contractor may submit a sample material risk disclosure statement to the commissioner for examination. If the commissioner approves of the statement or fails to respond within 30 days of receipt of the statement, the statement will be deemed to comply with this subdivision and with the plain language requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.91.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/517/#laws.0.2.0">1990 c 517 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/470/#laws.0.4.0">2000 c 470 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.92">
        <h1 class="shn">17.92 RECAPTURE OF CAPITAL INVESTMENT REQUIRED BY AN AGRICULTURAL CONTRACT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.92.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Notice and damages to be paid.</span></h2>
          <p>A contractor must not terminate or cancel a contract that requires a producer of agricultural commodities to make a capital investment in buildings or equipment that cost $100,000 or more and have a useful life of five or more years until:</p>
          <p>(1) the producer has been given written notice of the intention to terminate or cancel the contract at least 180 days before the effective date of the termination or cancellation or as provided in subdivision 3; and</p>
          <p>(2) the producer has been reimbursed for damages incurred by an investment in buildings or equipment that was made for the purpose of meeting minimum requirements of the contract.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.92.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notice when producer breaches contract.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivision 3, if a producer fails to comply with the provisions of a contract that requires a capital investment subject to subdivision 1, a contractor may not terminate or cancel that contract until:</p>
          <p>(1) the contractor has given written notice with all the reasons for the termination or cancellation at least 90 days before termination or cancellation or as provided in subdivision 3; and</p>
          <p>(2) the recipient of the notice fails to correct the reasons stated for termination or cancellation in the notice within 60 days of receipt of the notice.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.92.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Immediate effect of notice.</span></h2>
          <p>The 180-day notice period under subdivision 1, clause (1), and the 90-day notice period and 60-day notice period under subdivision 2, clauses (1) and (2), are waived and the contract may be canceled or terminated immediately if the alleged grounds for termination or cancellation are:</p>
          <p>(1) voluntary abandonment of the contract relationship by the producer; or</p>
          <p>(2) conviction of the producer of an offense directly related to the business conducted under the contract.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.92.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/517/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 517 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.93">
        <h1 class="shn">17.93 PARENT COMPANY RESPONSIBILITY FOR CONTRACTS OF SUBSIDIARIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.93.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Licensing.</span></h2>
          <p>If a contractor is required to obtain a license to purchase agricultural commodities, the licensing authority may require the parent company of a licensee subsidiary to guarantee payment or contract performance as a condition of licensing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.93.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Parent company liability.</span></h2>
          <p>If an agricultural contractor is the subsidiary of another corporation, partnership, or association, the parent corporation, partnership, or association is liable to a seller for the amount of any unpaid claim or contract performance claim if the contractor fails to pay or perform according to the terms of the contract.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.93.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/517/#laws.0.4.0">1990 c 517 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.94">
        <h1 class="shn">17.94 IMPLIED PROMISE OF GOOD FAITH.</h1>
        <p>There is an implied promise of good faith as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/336.1-201">336.1-201</a>(b)(20), by all parties in all agricultural contracts. In an action to recover damages, if the court finds that there has been a violation of this provision, damages, court costs, and attorney fees may be recovered.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.94.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/517/#laws.0.5.0">1990 c 517 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/162/#laws.3.1.0">2004 c 162 art 3 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.941">
        <h1 class="shn">17.941 PRODUCER'S RIGHT TO CANCEL.</h1>
        <p>A producer may cancel an agricultural contract by mailing a written cancellation notice to the contractor within three business days after the producer receives a copy of the signed contract, or before a later cancellation deadline if a later deadline is specified in the contract. The producer's right to cancel, the method by which the producer may cancel, and the deadline for canceling the contract shall be clearly disclosed in every agricultural contract.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.941.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/470/#laws.0.5.0">2000 c 470 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.942">
        <h1 class="shn">17.942 COVER SHEET REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.942.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Mandatory cover page.</span></h2>
          <p>An agricultural contract entered into or substantively amended after January 1, 2001, must contain as the first page, or first page of text if it is preceded by a title page or pages, a cover sheet as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.942.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>The cover sheet or sheets must comply with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>, and must contain the following:</p>
          <p>(1) a brief statement that the document is a legal contract between the contractor and the producer;</p>
          <p>(2) the statement "READ YOUR CONTRACT CAREFULLY. This cover sheet provides only a brief summary of your contract. This is not the contract and only the terms of the actual contract are legally binding. The contract itself sets forth, in detail, the rights and obligations of both you and the contractor. IT IS THEREFORE IMPORTANT THAT YOU READ YOUR CONTRACT CAREFULLY.";</p>
          <p>(3) the written disclosure of material risks required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.91#stat.17.91.2">17.91, subdivision 2</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) a statement detailing, in plain language, the producer's right to review the contract as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.941">17.941</a>; and</p>
          <p>(5) an index of the major provisions of the contract and the pages on which they are found, including:</p>
          <p>(i) the names of all parties to the contract;</p>
          <p>(ii) the definition sections of the contract;</p>
          <p>(iii) the provisions governing cancellation, renewal, or amendment of the contract by either party;</p>
          <p>(iv) the duties or obligations of each party; and</p>
          <p>(v) any provisions subject to change in the contract.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.942.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/470/#laws.0.6.0">2000 c 470 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.943">
        <h1 class="shn">17.943 CONTRACT FORMAT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.943.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Readability.</span></h2>
          <p>An agricultural contract must be in legible type, appropriately divided and captioned by its various sections, and written in clear and coherent language using words and grammar that are understandable by a person of average intelligence, education, and experience within the industry.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.943.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p>Subdivision 1 does not apply to particular words, phrases, provisions, or forms of agreement specifically required, recommended, or endorsed by a state or federal statute, rule, or regulation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.943.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Customarily used terms.</span></h2>
          <p>An agricultural contract may include technical terms to describe the services or property which are the subject of the contract, if the terms are customarily used by producers in the ordinary course of business in connection with the services or property being described.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.943.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/470/#laws.0.7.0">2000 c 470 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.944">
        <h1 class="shn">17.944 REVIEW BY COMMISSIONER.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.944.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Agricultural contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>, "agricultural contract" includes, where applicable, the cover sheet as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.942">17.942</a>, and material risk disclosure statement required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.91#stat.17.91.2">17.91, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.944.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Process of review.</span></h2>
          <p>A contractor may submit an agricultural contract to the commissioner for review as to whether it complies with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>. After reviewing the contract, the commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) certify that the contract complies with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) decline to certify that the contract complies with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a> and note objections;</p>
          <p>(3) decline to review the contract because the contract's compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a> is subject to pending litigation; or</p>
          <p>(4) decline to review the contract because the contract is not subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.944.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Factors in determining readability.</span></h2>
          <p>In determining whether a contract or cover sheet is readable within the meaning of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>, the commissioner shall consider at least the following factors:</p>
          <p>(1) the simplicity of the sentence structure;</p>
          <p>(2) the extent to which commonly used and understood words are employed;</p>
          <p>(3) the extent to which esoteric legal terms are avoided;</p>
          <p>(4) the extent to which references to other sections or provisions of the contract are minimized;</p>
          <p>(5) the Flesch scale analysis readability score as outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/72C.09">72C.09</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) the extent to which clear definitions are used in the text of the contract; and</p>
          <p>(7) additional factors relevant to the contract being easy to read and understand.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.944.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Process not reviewable.</span></h2>
          <p>Actions of the commissioner under subdivision 1 are not subject to chapter 14 and are not appealable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.944.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Limited effect of certification.</span></h2>
          <p>A contract certified under subdivision 1 is deemed to comply with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>. Certification of a contract under subdivision 1 does not constitute an approval of the contract's legality or legal effect.</p>
          <p>If the commissioner certifies a contract or fails to respond within 30 days of receipt of the contract, the contractor will have complied with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.91">17.91</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>, and the remedies stated in subdivisions 7 and 8 are not available.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.944.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Review not required.</span></h2>
          <p>Failure to submit a contract to the commissioner for review under subdivision 1 does not show a lack of good faith or raise a presumption that the contract violates section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.944.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Enforcement remedies.</span></h2>
          <p>A violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a> is a violation subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/8.31#stat.8.31.1">8.31, subdivision 1</a>. The remedies in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/8.31">8.31</a>, subdivisions 3 and 3a, are limited by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.9441">17.9441</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.944.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Reformation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In addition to the remedies provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/8.31">8.31</a>, a court reviewing an agricultural contract may change the terms of the contract or limit a provision to avoid an unfair result if it finds that:</p>
          <p>(1) a material provision of the contract violates section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) the violation caused the producer to be substantially confused about any of the rights, obligations, or remedies of the contract; and</p>
          <p>(3) the violation has caused or is likely to cause financial detriment to the producer.</p>
          <p>(b) If the court reforms or limits a provision of a contract, the court shall also make orders necessary to avoid unjust enrichment. Bringing a claim for relief under this subdivision does not entitle a producer to withhold performance of an otherwise valid contractual obligation. No relief may be granted under this subdivision unless the claim is brought before the obligations of the contract have been fully performed.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.944.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/470/#laws.0.8.0">2000 c 470 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.9441">
        <h1 class="shn">17.9441 LIMITS ON REMEDIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9441.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>In a proceeding in which civil penalties are claimed from a party for a violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>, it is a defense to the claim that the party made a good faith and reasonable effort to comply with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9441.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Attorney's fees.</span></h2>
          <p>A party who has made a good faith and reasonable effort to comply with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a> may not be assessed attorney's fees or costs of investigation in an action for violating section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9441.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Class action attorney's fees.</span></h2>
          <p>In a class action or series of class actions that arise from the use by a contractor of an agricultural contract found to violate section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a>, the amount of attorney's fees and costs of investigation assessed against that contractor and in favor of the class or classes may not exceed $10,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9441.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Limits on producer actions.</span></h2>
          <p>Violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a> is not a defense to a claim arising from a producer's breach of an agricultural contract. A producer may recover actual damages caused by a violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a> only if the violation caused the producer to not understand the rights, obligations, or remedies of the contract.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9441.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Statute of limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>A claim that an agricultural contract violates section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943">17.943</a> must be raised within six years of the date the contract is executed by the producer.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.9441.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/470/#laws.0.9.0">2000 c 470 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.9442">
        <h1 class="shn">17.9442 APPLICABILITY OF CONTRACT REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <p>The requirements for the written disclosure of material risks under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.91#stat.17.91.2">17.91, subdivision 2</a>; the three-day review period under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.941">17.941</a>; the cover sheet requirement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.942">17.942</a>; and the contract readability requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.943#stat.17.943.1">17.943, subdivision 1</a>, do not apply to contracts which provide for:</p>
        <p>(1) the sale and purchase of a fixed amount of a commodity for delivery at a set price;</p>
        <p>(2) cash sale and forward delivery grain contracts for grain meeting certain specifications and does not prescribe the time, manner, and nature of planting, cultivating, and harvesting the crop producing the grain, including fixed price forward contracts, minimum price contracts, mini-max contracts, basis contracts, hedge-to-arrive contracts, and delayed price contracts;</p>
        <p>(3) contracts agreed to between a processor and an accredited bargaining organization under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.691">17.691</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.702">17.702</a>;</p>
        <p>(4) futures contracts which involve the sale or purchase of a standardized quantity of a commodity for future delivery on a regulated commodity exchange;</p>
        <p>(5) agricultural marketing contracts between a capital stock cooperative and its members under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/308A.205">308A.205</a>; or</p>
        <p>(6) occasional sales between persons who produce or cause to be produced food, feed, or fiber in a quantity beyond their own family use.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.9442.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/470/#laws.0.10.0">2000 c 470 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.57.0">2001 c 161 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/373/#laws.0.6.0">2002 c 373 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.9443">
        <h1 class="shn">17.9443 WAIVER OF CONTRACT PROVISIONS IS VOID.</h1>
        <p>Any provision of an agricultural contract which waives or attempts to waive any provision of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.90">17.90</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.97">17.97</a> is void.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.9443.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/470/#laws.0.11.0">2000 c 470 s 11</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.945">
        <h1 class="shn">17.945 RULES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may adopt rules to implement sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.90">17.90</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.98">17.98</a>, including the prohibition of specific trade practices.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.945.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/517/#laws.0.6.0">1990 c 517 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.95">
        <h1 class="shn">17.95 DEPARTMENT OF AGRICULTURE OMBUDSMAN.</h1>
        <p>A position is created in the Department of Agriculture to provide information, investigate complaints arising from this chapter, and provide or facilitate dispute resolutions.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.95.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/517/#laws.0.7.0">1990 c 517 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.97">
        <h1 class="shn">17.97 AGRICULTURAL INPUT PREPAYMENTS.</h1>
        <p>If a producer makes a prepayment for agricultural production inputs that include but are not limited to seed, feed, fertilizer, pesticides, or fuel for future delivery, the producer may demand a letter of credit or bank guarantee from the provider of the inputs to ensure reimbursement if delivery does not occur.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.97.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/517/#laws.0.8.0">1990 c 517 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.98">
        <h1 class="shn">17.98 DAIRY MARKETING CONTRACTS EXCEPTED.</h1>
        <p>Dairy marketing agreements between producers and purchasers of milk are not contracts for purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.90">17.90</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.98">17.98</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.98.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/517/#laws.0.9.0">1990 c 517 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ENFORCEMENT OF AGRICULTURAL LAWS</h1>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.981"><b>17.981</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.982">
        <h1 class="shn">17.982 CRIMINAL AND ADMINISTRATIVE PENALTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.982.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Criminal penalties.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A person who violates a provision of chapter 25 or 31B for which a penalty has not been prescribed is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.982.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Administrative penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may, as an alternative to misdemeanor prosecution, impose an administrative penalty on a person who violates a statute or rule enforceable by the commissioner. For a first violation, the commissioner may impose an administrative penalty of not more than $1,000 for each violation. For a second or succeeding violation, the commissioner may impose an administrative penalty of not more than $1,500 for each violation. Each day a violation continues is a separate violation.</p>
          <p>(b) In determining the amount of the administrative penalty the commissioner may consider:</p>
          <p>(1) the willfulness of the violation;</p>
          <p>(2) the gravity of the violation;</p>
          <p>(3) the person's history of past violations;</p>
          <p>(4) the number of violations;</p>
          <p>(5) the economic benefit from the violation; and</p>
          <p>(6) other factors identified in the commissioner's citation.</p>
          <p>(c) For a second or succeeding violation, the commissioner shall determine the amount of a penalty by considering the factors in paragraph (b) and:</p>
          <p>(1) similarity between the violations;</p>
          <p>(2) time elapsed since the last violation; and</p>
          <p>(3) the person's response to the most recent violation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.982.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/316/#laws.0.2.0">1991 c 316 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.34.0">1999 c 231 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.11.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/45/#laws.1.20.0">2007 c 45 art 1 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/244/#laws.2.1.0">2012 c 244 art 2 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.983">
        <h1 class="shn">17.983 ADMINISTRATIVE PENALTIES AND ENFORCEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.983.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Administrative penalties; citation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">If a person has violated a provision of chapter 25 or 31B, the commissioner may issue a written citation to the person by personal service or by certified mail. The citation must describe the nature of the violation and the statute or rule alleged to have been violated; state the time for correction, if applicable; and the amount of any proposed fine. The citation must advise the person to notify the commissioner in writing within 30 days if the person wishes to appeal the citation. If the person fails to appeal the citation, the citation is the final order and not subject to further review.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.983.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.98.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.983.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Contested case.</span></h2>
          <p>If a person appeals a citation or a penalty assessment within the time limits in subdivision 1, the commissioner shall initiate a contested proceeding under chapter 14.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.983.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/316/#laws.0.3.0">1991 c 316 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/65/#laws.0.2.0">1993 c 65 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.35.0">1999 c 231 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.1.12.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 1 s 12</a>,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/45/#laws.1.21.0">2007 c 45 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/244/#laws.2.2.0">2012 c 244 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/88/#laws.3.2.0">2017 c 88 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/43/#laws.2.13.0">2023 c 43 art 2 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.984"><b>17.984</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/43/#laws.2.142.0">2023 c 43 art 2 s 142</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PASSING ON THE FARM CENTER</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.985">
        <h1 class="shn">17.985 PASSING ON THE FARM CENTER.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.985.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose; objectives.</span></h2>
          <p>The Passing on the Farm Center is established as a part of Southwest Technical College in Granite Falls to assist individuals beginning farming and family farming operations. The center shall also assist in facilitating the transition of farming operations from established farmers to beginning farmers by creating and maintaining an information base inventorying land and facilities available for acquisition and bringing them together to increase the number of family farming operations in this state. The objectives of the center include, but are not limited to, the following:</p>
          <p>(1) using the services of a certified public accountant, real estate agents, and attorneys to provide education in estate planning and farm transfer programs for interested retiring farmers;</p>
          <p>(2) assessing needs of beginning farmers and retiring farmers in order to identify program and service opportunities including developing statewide apprenticeship programs between beginning and retiring farmers; and</p>
          <p>(3) developing, coordinating, and delivering statewide through Southwest Technical College in Granite Falls and other entities, as appropriate, targeted education to beginning farmers and retiring farm families.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.985.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Programs and services.</span></h2>
          <p>Programs and services provided by the center must include, but are not limited to, the development of skills and knowledge in farm estate planning and other topics related to intergenerational farm transfer. The center shall develop and distribute a detailed questionnaire for interested retired farmers and landowners and beginning farmers for the purpose of connecting them with each other and to develop computerized lists. The center shall coordinate to the extent practicable with agricultural information centers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.985.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Annual report.</span></h2>
          <p>The center shall submit a report annually to the legislature on or before February 1. The report shall include, but is not limited to, recommendations for methods by which more individuals may be encouraged to enter agriculture.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.985.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/220/#laws.0.41.0">1995 c 220 s 41</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ENTRY INTO FARM ANIMAL FACILITIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.986">
        <h1 class="shn">17.986 ENTRY INTO FARM ANIMAL FACILITIES.</h1>
        <p>No law enforcement, peace, or animal control officer may enter a facility where confined farm animals are kept unless the officer follows a procedure and directive for biosecurity measures that are identified by the commissioner of natural resources and the Board of Animal Health.</p>
        <p>This section does not apply to emergency or exigent circumstances.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.986.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/55/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 55 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.17.987"><b>17.987</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.162.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 162</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">WATER QUALITY CERTIFICATION PROGRAM</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.9891">
        <h1 class="shn">17.9891 PURPOSE.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner, in consultation with the commissioner of natural resources, commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency, and Board of Water and Soil Resources, may implement a Minnesota agricultural water quality certification program whereby a producer who demonstrates practices and management sufficient to protect water quality is certified for up to ten years and presumed to be contributing the producer's share of any targeted reduction of water pollutants during the certification period. The program is voluntary. The voluntary program will first be piloted in selected watersheds across the state, until the commissioner, in consultation with the Minnesota Agricultural Water Quality Certification Program Advisory Committee, commissioner of natural resources, commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency, and Board of Water and Soil Resources, determines the program is suitable to be implemented in other watersheds.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.9891.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.2.5.0">2013 c 114 art 2 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.9892">
        <h1 class="shn">17.9892 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9892.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.9891">17.9891</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.993">17.993</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9892.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Certification.</span></h2>
          <p>"Certification" means a producer has demonstrated compliance with all applicable environmental rules and statutes for all of the producer's owned and rented agricultural land and has achieved a satisfactory score through the certification instrument as verified by a certifying agent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9892.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Certifying agent.</span></h2>
          <p>"Certifying agent" means a person who is authorized by the commissioner to assess producers to determine whether a producer satisfies the standards of the program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9892.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Effective control.</span></h2>
          <p>"Effective control" means possession of land by ownership, written lease, or other legal agreement and authority to act as decision-maker for the day-to-day management of the operation at the time the producer achieves certification and for the required certification period.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9892.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Eligible land.</span></h2>
          <p>"Eligible land" means all acres of a producer's agricultural operation, whether contiguous or not, that are under the effective control of the producer at the time the producer enters into the program and that the producer operates with equipment, labor, and management.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9892.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Program.</span></h2>
          <p>"Program" means the Minnesota agricultural water quality certification program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9892.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Technical assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>"Technical assistance" means professional, advisory, or cost-share assistance provided to individuals in order to achieve certification.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.9892.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.2.6.0">2013 c 114 art 2 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.9893">
        <h1 class="shn">17.9893 CERTIFICATION INSTRUMENT.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner, in consultation with the Minnesota Agricultural Water Quality Certification Program Advisory Committee, commissioner of natural resources, commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency, and Board of Water and Soil Resources, shall develop an analytical instrument to assess the water quality practices and management of agricultural operations. This instrument shall be used to certify that the water quality practices and management of an agricultural operation are consistent with state water quality goals and standards. The commissioner shall define a satisfactory score for certification purposes. The certification instrument tool shall:</p>
        <p>(1) integrate applicable existing regulatory requirements;</p>
        <p>(2) utilize technology and prioritize ease of use;</p>
        <p>(3) utilize a water quality index or score applicable to the landscape;</p>
        <p>(4) incorporate a process for updates and revisions as practices, management, and technology changes become established and approved; and</p>
        <p>(5) comprehensively address water quality impacts.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.9893.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.2.7.0">2013 c 114 art 2 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.9894">
        <h1 class="shn">17.9894 CERTIFYING AGENT LICENSE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9894.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who offers certification services to producers as part of the program must satisfy all criteria in subdivision 2 and be licensed by the commissioner. A certifying agent is ineligible to provide certification services to any producer to whom the certifying agent has also provided technical assistance. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1283">16A.1283</a>, the commissioner may set license fees.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9894.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Certifying agent requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>In order to be licensed as a certifying agent, a person must:</p>
          <p>(1) be an agricultural conservation professional employed by a soil and water conservation district or the Natural Resources Conservation Service, a Minnesota certified crop advisor recognized by the American Society of Agronomy, or an individual with agricultural conservation experience approved by the commissioner. The commissioner may establish eligibility criteria by rule;</p>
          <p>(2) have passed a comprehensive exam, as set by the commissioner, evaluating knowledge of water quality, soil health, best farm management techniques, and the certification instrument; and</p>
          <p>(3) maintain continuing education requirements as set by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.9894.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.2.8.0">2013 c 114 art 2 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.9895">
        <h1 class="shn">17.9895 DUTIES OF A CERTIFYING AGENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9895.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>A certifying agent shall conduct a formal certification assessment utilizing the certification instrument to determine whether a producer meets program criteria. If a producer satisfies all requirements, the certifying agent shall notify the commissioner of the producer's eligibility and request that the commissioner issue a certificate. All records and documents used in the assessment shall be compiled by the certifying agent and submitted to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9895.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Violations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In the event a certifying agent violates any provision of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.9891">17.9891</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.993">17.993</a> or an order of the commissioner, the commissioner may issue a written warning or a correction order and may suspend or revoke a license.</p>
          <p>(b) If the commissioner suspends or revokes a license, the certifying agent has ten days from the date of suspension or revocation to appeal. If a certifying agent appeals, the commissioner shall hold an administrative hearing within 30 days of the suspension or revocation of the license, or longer by agreement of the parties, to determine whether the license is revoked or suspended. The commissioner shall issue an opinion within 30 days. If a person notifies the commissioner that the person intends to contest the commissioner's opinion, the Office of Administrative Hearings shall conduct a hearing in accordance with the applicable provisions of chapter 14 for hearings in contested cases.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.9895.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.2.9.0">2013 c 114 art 2 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.9896">
        <h1 class="shn">17.9896 CERTIFICATION PROCEDURES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9896.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Producer duties.</span></h2>
          <p>A producer who seeks certification of eligible land shall conduct an initial assessment using the certification instrument, obtain technical assistance if necessary to achieve a satisfactory score on the certification instrument, and apply for certification from a licensed certifying agent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9896.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Owned land.</span></h2>
          <p>Once certified, if a producer obtains ownership of additional agricultural land, the producer must notify a certifying agent and obtain certification of the additional land within one year in order to retain the producer's original certification.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9896.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Leased land.</span></h2>
          <p>Once certified, if a producer leases additional land, then the producer must notify a certifying agent before farming operations commence on the newly leased land. A producer who operates leased land is not required to implement practices that permanently alter the landscape in order to be certified or remain certified if the land is added following the original certification. A producer who operates leased land must demonstrate sufficient annual crop management practices, consistent with the original certification agreement, in order to remain certified.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.17.9896.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Violations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may revoke a certification if the producer violates subdivision 2 or 3.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may revoke a certification and seek reimbursement of any monetary benefit a producer may have received due to certification from a producer who fails to maintain certification criteria.</p>
          <p>(c) If the commissioner revokes a certification, the producer has 30 days from the date of suspension or revocation to appeal. If a producer appeals, the commissioner shall hold an administrative hearing within 30 days of the suspension or revocation of the certification, or longer by agreement of the parties, to determine whether the certification is revoked or suspended. The commissioner shall issue an opinion within 30 days. If the producer notifies the commissioner that the producer intends to contest the commissioner's opinion, the Office of Administrative Hearings shall conduct a hearing in accordance with the applicable provisions of chapter 14 for hearings in contested cases.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.9896.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.2.10.0">2013 c 114 art 2 s 10</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.9897">
        <h1 class="shn">17.9897 CERTIFICATION CERTAINTY.</h1>
        <p>(a) Once a producer is certified, the producer:</p>
        <p>(1) retains certification for up to ten years from the date of certification if the producer complies with the certification agreement, even if the producer does not comply with new state water protection laws or rules that take effect during the certification period;</p>
        <p>(2) is presumed to be meeting the producer's contribution to any targeted reduction of pollutants during the certification period;</p>
        <p>(3) is required to continue implementation of practices that maintain the producer's certification; and</p>
        <p>(4) is required to retain all records pertaining to certification.</p>
        <p>(b) Paragraph (a) does not preclude enforcement of a local rule or ordinance by a local unit of government.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.9897.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.2.11.0">2013 c 114 art 2 s 11</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.9898">
        <h1 class="shn">17.9898 AUDITS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall perform random audits of producers and certifying agents to ensure compliance with the program. All producers and certifying agents shall cooperate with the commissioner during these audits, and provide all relevant documents to the commissioner for inspection and copying. Any delay, obstruction, or refusal to cooperate with the commissioner's audit or falsification of or failure to provide required data or information is a violation subject to the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.9895#stat.17.9895.2">17.9895, subdivision 2</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.9896#stat.17.9896.4">17.9896, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.9898.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.2.12.0">2013 c 114 art 2 s 12</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.9899">
        <h1 class="shn">17.9899 DATA.</h1>
        <p>All data collected under the program that identifies a producer or a producer's location are considered nonpublic data as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.9">13.02, subdivision 9</a>, or private data on individuals as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.12">13.02, subdivision 12</a>. The commissioner shall make available summary data of program outcomes on data classified as private or nonpublic under this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.9899.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.2.13.0">2013 c 114 art 2 s 13</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.991">
        <h1 class="shn">17.991 RULEMAKING.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may adopt rules to implement the program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.991.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.2.14.0">2013 c 114 art 2 s 14</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.992">
        <h1 class="shn">17.992 REPORTS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner, in consultation with the Minnesota Agricultural Water Quality Certification Program Advisory Committee, commissioner of natural resources, commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency, and Board of Water and Soil Resources, shall issue a biennial report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over agricultural policy on the status of the program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.992.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.2.15.0">2013 c 114 art 2 s 15</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.17.993">
        <h1 class="shn">17.993 FINANCIAL ASSISTANCE.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may use contributions from gifts or other state accounts, provided that the purpose of the expenditure is consistent with the purpose of the accounts, for grants, loans, or other financial assistance.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.17.993.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.2.16.0">2013 c 114 art 2 s 16</a>
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
